# Run these when you're home

Ethan, 2026-09-09, from work: *"please save all the code you need me too
run for when I'm home."*

Short list, in order. Everything is copy-paste. **Block 1 is the only one
that changes anything** — the rest are read-only and just tell me what
the box is actually doing, which is the half I cannot see from here.

Paste the output back with the block number and I can carry on.

The long history of one-off checks lives in `DROPLET_CHECKS.md`; this
file is only what is outstanding right now, and lines get deleted from it
as they are done. Pruned to that on 2026-10-02 (audit #13): every
answered or finished block moved there, word for word.

Real's first screens before the sign-up, with our real Record on the last, and every big play's card like Real's: a 0–10 rating from the win chance it moved, a go-ahead, tie or walk-off tag, the players' lines so far, the season and night counts, the win chance before and after, and a share (step 182; DY). Referral codes: every member's code gives a friend $6.25 off their first payment and the member $6.25 off their next bill when the friend pays (a quarter of a month, as an amount on every plan), with a link, a share, and how it's gone (step 183; DZ). Right after sign-up, Real's next screen: follow your teams and players, each one a watch, so members hear when a pick on them posts and free accounts hear only how many; a new account lands on Live, not the plans (step 184; EA). Live injuries and birthdays on the game: a status that came onto ESPN's injury list during the game, under the score with the time, and a player's birthday from every league's roster feed (step 185; EB). Sign up or in with a phone number and a texted code, no password, through Twilio Verify; off until you set its three values, US and Canadian numbers only, with caps per number and address (step 186; EC). Each live game's win chance drawn play by play, the big swings dotted, from the feed's own number (step 187; ED). Your players, live: each player you follow, his line right now from whichever game he is in, on one strip at the top of Live (step 188; EE). Big moments by push: a walk-off, a late go-ahead or any play rated 8.5+ in a game of a team or player you follow, the score and who did it, a tap into the game (step 189; EF). Call it: on every live game, call how the next at-bat, drive, basket or shot ends, graded off the game's own file, with your streak and the room's best (step 190; EG). Your ticket in the game's chat: your saved parlay's legs in that game with the box's number so far, and a Share (step 191; EH). Your players on the iPhone widget and lock screen, by a key the app hands the widget; and the calendar link named in the privacy policy and deleted with the account (step 192; EI). The game chat out of the corner and rebuilt: a card under every game's scoreboard, a bar on a phone, docked under the header on a computer, messages grouped like a chat app with the tools on hover or a tap (step 193; EJ). While you were away (what you missed when you come back to a live game) and the game in 30 seconds (a finished game's story on one card, with a Share) (step 194; EK). Watching on TV: a delay under every live game holds the page and its chat back 10, 30 or 60 seconds so nothing spoils your TV (step 195; EL). An account to use the site, like Real's first screen, with the paid side shown on it: tonight's picks locked with their counts, Free beside Member, the last graded pick won or lost, the plans and the trial's days; the offer once after a new account's teams; and from the free site, Get picks in the top bar, a members strip on Live and locked tabs that say what they hold (step 196; EM). The game chat's buttons fixed (a name clash from step 196 broke the chat sheet and My Bets once the chat loaded; the chat card no longer rebuilds under your thumb), and the plans page leading with the free side: Free account beside Member, Sign up free, and a plan chosen before an account carried through the sign-up (step 197; EN). The free trial's reminder: one email about a day before the trial turns into the $25 charge, with when, what and how to cancel, and the same line on Live while it runs (step 198; EO). What a lapsed member missed: the plans page's notice carries the public record since their plan ended, win or lose (step 199; EP). How many are here: the first screen counts accounts made this week and people in game rooms right now, real or not shown (step 200; EQ). Not today: the plans page's button for the free picks-are-up alert, with its own Turn off (step 201; ER). The house posts the big moments: a play rated 8 or more goes into its game's chat from the site's own account, on the play's thread (step 202; ES). Call it across every game: the week's and season's ladders, a flame beside a hot caller's name in the chats, and a share at five in a row (step 203; ET). Final scores by push: a new Alerts switch sends the final, the play of the game and the best line when a followed team's game ends (step 204; EU). The app replaces its old copy on your phone: a new version takes over on opening, a refresh pill mid-use, a refresh on coming back, and an error on an old copy reloads by itself (step 205; EV). Tonight's commands, in order (EW). Every sport's game scoreboard is the basketball page's: football, college and baseball open on the team-colour board with the crests, the city over the name, the big score with the ball beside the side that has it, and the clock, the down or the inning in the middle (step 206; EX). On a live game page the sport buttons hop between games: another league's button opens that league's game in progress (its latest final when none is on, the Live tab on it when neither), and the row lights the game's own league (step 207; EY). The Fantasy room, round one: a Fantasy 101 card for anyone new to the game, every team defense projected (the lineup can finally choose one, and the pickups name one to stream), a to-do list at the top of your league desk, and the pickups that help your own roster with a FAAB bid and the player to drop (step 208; EZ). Start A or B?: two to four players side by side, how often each outscores the other, and when a questionable player's status is known against the other's kickoff, on the Fantasy page and in Ask (step 209; FA). Your season, on a linked Sleeper league's desk: your playoff chance from the rest of the season played 2,000 times on your league's own schedule, what record gets you in, power rankings with the all-play record and luck, and last week in review: points left on your bench, your costliest and best calls, and the league's awards (step 210; FB). Lineup alerts: turn on "My fantasy lineup" and your phone hears when a starter you still have in is out, on a bye or doubtful (step 211; FC). Your matchup, live: during games your Sleeper desk opens on the score as it stands, each side's projected final and the win chance, slot by slot, refreshing every minute; and every football game page lists its top fantasy scorers (step 212; FD). NBA and NHL tonight in fantasy points: a Fantasy tab with every player on each night's basketball and hockey boards in daily-fantasy points, from the same projections as the props (step 213; FE). The Fantasy room redesigned like the fantasy apps: My team first, rooms as icon tiles, one line for the week, player rows with position colours and range bars, buy-low and sell-high as rows with bars, the head-to-head's workings folded, and no brown wash down long cards (step 214; FF). The whole app, pass one: subtitles as plain lines instead of gold-ruled blocks, the stripe only on graded cards, no stadium strip on a phone, sentence type for row details and the Record's buttons, and the Edge page opening with its name (step 215; FG). Ask Qellys shows its work: the lookups it is making appear as it makes them, an answer about a player's line carries his hit rates and last ten games as bars, a price question every book's price with the best marked, record and start/sit cards, and three next questions under every answer (step 216; FH). Act on an Ask answer: under the card about tonight's player line, Add to slip, Track it (followed on the Live tab, settled off the box score, "your bet is on" on your phone) and Watch; a ticket of one leg is a bet, never "down to its last leg" (step 217; FI). The lights on: every league its own colour on its button and as a glow at the top of the page, the home's doors in four colours, Most Likely's tiers in three with the night's best pick in a moving rim, pages that rise in as they open, gold buttons that glow, and Ask Qellys with a sheen (step 218; FJ). Ask reads your open bets when you attach them (each graded live, the way the Live tab does, and never cached for anyone else), and an Ask button on the fantasy player card (step 219; FK). The board comes alive: how often he cleared tonight's number (last 5, last 10, every game) on every prop row, confetti when a bet of yours wins, every game card in its teams' colours, and a saved ticket's numbers that tick on the Live tab (step 220; FL). Price alerts from Ask's price card ("Josh Allen under at -105 or better", on Alerts and your phone) and your fantasy starters on tonight's board (step 221; FM). The blend: one typeface for words and numbers, with the numbers lined up in columns, a warm graphite ground, softer colours, fills instead of outlines, rounder corners and softer glows, the league colours as one family and the venue photos toned to match (step 222; FN). Nine more ideas: what each live leg of a saved ticket still needs, hot streaks on prop rows, our parlay into your slip in one tap, the line's path on Ask's price card, Ask about your own teams and players, Track it filling in your stake, "What brings you here?" at sign-up, keyboard shortcuts on a computer, and your week by push on Monday (step 223; FO). No hard lines: every outline a fill, a chosen chip or league a tint and its own light, the top bar fading into the page, a team's colour spilling from it instead of a stripe, and the dividers that remain a whisper (step 224; FP). The dashboard's newest draw wins: an older draw that finishes late no longer puts back a Live now card the newer board hid, or the last league's (step 225; FQ). The last lines: badges, chips and the chosen plan as tints and glows instead of rings, the stripes down bet, pick and note cards as light from the left edge, an open tab a tinted pill, and no outline back on hover; and what the blend broke, fixed: the desktop bar running off the screen at 1280px, the squeezed logo, team names cut on the scoreboards, the light theme's gold buttons (step 226; FR). The room against the math: the game chat's who-wins vote through the game on the win-chance chart, and where the room and the math were furthest apart (step 227; FS). Spoiler-free later: a game saved for later shows no score anywhere on the device, its page a veil until "Show the game" (step 228; FT). Your fantasy lineup, live: your Sleeper matchup's score and win chance at the top of Live, and your starters' lines with their fantasy points on Your players (step 229; FU). Trivia in the stoppages: at a timeout, a break or between innings, one question off the two teams' season logs, four names to tap, the answer and the room's split after; and the Call it buttons drawn as buttons again (step 230; FV). Watch parties: a private thread inside a game's chat for your friends, by invite link (step 231; FW). Crew watch: an NFL game's flags so far against its referee's games, and a baseball game's plate umpire, call by call (step 232; FX). Yearly at month two: a monthly member's third bill brings one offer of the yearly plan on Stripe's own page, off until you switch on plan changes in Stripe's portal and QB_YEARLY_OFFER (step 233; FY). Gift a month: $25 once for a friend, delivered as a code, off until you name a gift price in Stripe (step 234; FZ). Passkeys: sign in with Face ID, Touch ID or the phone's own lock, added in Settings, nothing to switch on (step 235; GA). What you circled on the Home: the game tile's hard lines gone (the teams' colours one layer over the whole tile), the pick numbers in pills that fit them, and the iPhone app's top bar solid, with the strip under the clock holding still while the bar slides away (step 236; GB). Each Most Likely tier in its colour wherever it is named: Top picks green, Strong gold, Worth a look sky, on the labels by game, the section titles, the chips and a light down each card (step 237; GC).
The whole picture, sport by sport, is in `docs/ALL_SPORTS_GAPS_2026-10-13.md`.
All of it is on the box already; it pulls every five minutes. Lettered,
so it never clashes with the numbered steps further down that every note
refers to. Paste back what each one prints, with its letter.

**BL first, if you do one thing** (step 116): it runs every check below
that can run on any day, in one go. **If you only do a few, do these, in this order.** AM first if the
boards are still empty an hour after the fix lands (it reads why). Then A
and B: is today's code there, and is the box well; AS beside them says how
long a sweep takes and where its minutes go. Then the four that matter before
the NBA's opening night on the 20th: N (the club rosters and the injury
board, read on the box; seconds); AG's first two commands (last season's
steals, blocks and turnovers, about half an hour at low priority, then
the defence measurement); AI once AG's first has finished (the NBA's fits
on its own model, about ten minutes: the fits for the three counts read
the games AG adds, and AI's second command is AG's third, so run that one
once, there); and X's first command (the shot chart's field names,
checked against a real game while there is time to fix them). I is the
one a fix is waiting on: the MLB profit calendar stays stuck at September
until I see what it prints. C and D are
the only two that need you to switch something on (email, Stripe).
Everything else is a read-only check: any order, any night; paste what it
prints.

Nothing here changes anything except C (only if email isn't on yet),
D's Stripe settings, Q's photo fill (photo addresses only — the same
thing tomorrow's chores would write), AD's two model files (the table
the chores would fit anyway, and the test's verdict) and AG's backfill
(last season's NBA box scores read again, adding the three stats beside
the rows already there) and its rank fit (the store Wednesday's chores
write anyway), and AI's refit (the NBA's fitted model files, the ones
Wednesday's refit writes anyway), and AP's fill if you start it (the hit
page's tables, the rows tonight's chores write anyway), and AR's probe (it
downloads any Savant file over six hours old, as the next MLB build would),
and AZ's cap if you move it (one setting and a restart).
Every command runs on the box (`ssh` in first).

**A. The box has today's code.** The first line should end `The settle lane counts as alive while it runs; college flags carry their team`
(or be newer), and the second should print `has today's code`. If it prints nothing or
"Not a valid commit name", the box hasn't pulled yet: wait five minutes
and run both again.
```
git -C /srv/qellys log --oneline -1
git -C /srv/qellys merge-base --is-ancestor 05270b4b HEAD && echo "has today's code"
```

**B. Health check** (paste all of it):
```
uptime; free -m
ps -eo pid,ni,pcpu,pmem,etime,args --sort=-pcpu | head -8
for p in / /js/app.js /data/recommendations.json /data/record.json; do curl -s -o /dev/null -w "$p %{http_code} %{size_download}B %{time_total}s\n" http://127.0.0.1:8000$p; done
journalctl -u qellys --since "2 hours ago" --no-pager | grep -iE "refresh|cycle|took|build|slip watch" | grep -v "/api/" | tail -15
```

**C. Is email on?** "Forgot password?" and the new last-leg email both
need it:
```
curl -s http://127.0.0.1:8000/api/account/me; echo
```
`"reset_by_email": true` means it's on; go to D. `false` means step 26
below isn't done yet. It takes about ten minutes: a Resend account, its
key put on the box with `sudo ./deploy/setenv.sh QB_RESEND_KEY`, a
restart, then the test email in 26 D. Until then the Forgot page sends
people to qellysbook@gmail.com, and the Live tab says email isn't
switched on instead of offering the last-leg switch.

**D. Stripe** (step 37 says why). Three settings in Stripe's dashboard,
nothing to type on the box:
* Developers → Webhooks → your endpoint → **Send test webhook**. It
  should say `200`. If it's blocked, Bot Fight Mode is on; docs/DEPLOY.md
  says how to turn it off.
* Settings → Billing → **Customer portal**: "Update payment methods" and
  "Invoice history" switched on. That's how a locked customer pays.
* Settings → Billing → **Subscriptions and emails**: "Send emails when
  card payments fail" switched on.

Then two read-only checks. They print statuses, counts and times, never
an email. Open the site on a subscribed account first; the second line
should then print under 60 (seconds since Stripe was last asked):
```
cd /srv/qellys && sudo -u qellys python3 -c "import time; from engine import accounts as A, billing as B; c=A.connect(); B.init(c); n=time.time(); print([tuple(r) for r in c.execute('SELECT status, COUNT(*), SUM(checked_at IS NOT NULL), ROUND(MAX(?-checked_at)/3600.0,1) FROM subscriptions GROUP BY status', (n,))])"
cd /srv/qellys && sudo -u qellys python3 -c "import time; from engine import accounts as A, billing as B; c=A.connect(); B.init(c); print(c.execute('SELECT ROUND(?-MAX(checked_at)) FROM subscriptions', (time.time(),)).fetchone()[0])"
```
In the first, each tuple is (status, accounts, read from Stripe, hours
since the oldest read). Every status except `canceled` and `none` should
have the third number equal to the second, and the last under 6.

**E. The parlay checker's measurements** (read-only, a few minutes each;
paste back the tables). Steps 38, 39 and 41 say how to read them:
```
cd /srv/qellys && sudo -u qellys python3 -m engine.proplivefit
cd /srv/qellys && sudo -u qellys python3 -m engine.slipread nfl "Josh Allen" rush_att over 5.5 -130
cd /srv/qellys && sudo -u qellys python3 -m engine.slipreadfit --sport nfl --seasons 2023 2024 2025
cd /srv/qellys && sudo -u qellys python3 -m engine.slipreadfit --sport cfb --seasons 2024 2025 --limit 3000
```
* The first replays the live chance on three NFL seasons. If "blend"
  beats "ours" by much, say so; changing the model is your call.
* The second is our read on a prop no board carries. `"found": "read"`
  with a projection means it works.
* The last two replay that read on seasons already played. "ours" should
  beat "last8" on Brier (lower is better) in every market, and each
  calibration band should land near what it said.

**F. Walk it on your phone.**
1. In Ask, tap the paperclip, then **Grade a parlay screenshot**, with any
   slip.
2. On the card, check the grade, "Why we say X%" under a leg, any leg our
   model reads off the board, the better legs, the hedge and the
   cash-out box.
3. Type a stake and tap **Save to My Bets**.
4. Open the Live tab. The slip is under **Your saved slips**, and the
   email switch sits above it. Tick it if C said email is on.

**G. After a game night with a saved slip open** (step 44). The first
prints a line for each minute a slip reached its last leg; the second
lists every alert noted, and `mailed` 1 means the email went:
```
journalctl -u qellys --since "12 hours ago" --no-pager | grep "slip watch"
cd /srv/qellys && sudo -u qellys python3 -c "from engine import accounts as A; c = A.connect(); print([tuple(r) for r in c.execute('SELECT user_id, bet_id, fired_at, mailed FROM slip_alerts')])"
```
During an NHL game, the new hockey box file (step 38). It prints when it
was written, each game, and how many player lines it has:
```
cd /srv/qellys && sudo -u qellys python3 -c "import json; d=json.load(open('web/data/live_nhl_box.json')); print(d['generated_at'], [(g['away']+'@'+g['home'], g['state'], len(g['players'])) for g in d['games']])"
```

*Ideas, not built.* Say yes to any; each is measured before it moves a
number, as everything else was.
* **MLB, for next season** (measure over the winter; the NFL stays
  first). RBI, runs, hits + runs + RBI, walks and stolen bases: all five
  are in the game log the box already pulls every night, and each would
  cost odds credits. From Savant, the site we already read: sprint speed
  and catcher pop time for stolen bases, team defence for hits allowed,
  bat speed and squared-up rate (2024 on). Travel and rest, from the
  schedule and ballparks we already hold.
* **Chart the MLB season sooner.** The box fills 400 games a night on
  purpose; with memory capped now (step 81) it could fill more, at the
  risk of MLB slowing our requests.
* **Fresher boards.** A sweep of every board takes about half an hour on
  the box, so a board is normally up to that old (step 88). Once AS shows
  where the minutes go, the slowest builds can be trimmed, or a league
  with no game for a day rebuilt less often, which leaves more of each
  sweep to the leagues playing.

**I. Why the MLB profit calendar stops at September** (step 45 says how
to read it; read-only, paste all of it):

after reading the finals (none until the first night after pulling); the
second fits it now instead, the same file the chores would write, and
prints how many balls it was fitted on; the third runs the test on every
stored plate appearance and saves its verdict (add `--dry-run` to print
without saving); the fourth asks for a hitter's chart and prints his
average, expected average, slugging, expected slugging and the verdict
the chart now reads:
```
ls -la /srv/qellys/data/models/mlb_xstats.json
cd /srv/qellys && sudo -u qellys python3 -c "from engine import db; from engine.mlb import xstats as x; m=x.fit_from_db(db.connect()); print(m['n'], x.save(m) if m['n'] >= x.MIN_BALLS else 'too few balls to fit')"
cd /srv/qellys && sudo -u qellys nice -n 19 python3 -m engine.mlb.xstatsfit
curl -s "http://127.0.0.1:8000/api/players/chart?kind=spray&sport=mlb&player=Aaron%20Judge" | python3 -c "import json,sys; d=json.load(sys.stdin); L=d.get('season_line') or {}; print(d.get('player'), d.get('season'), L.get('avg'), L.get('xba'), L.get('slg'), L.get('xslg'), d.get('x_measured'))"
```
The third prints the balls the table was fitted on, the three slices'
dates and sizes, then for HITS and BASES the actual, expected and league
scores on the last slice with each half's gain against the bar and
`PASSES the bar` or `does not pass`. Paste all of it back: a pass is
written on the chart and is your call from there; it moves no price by
itself. Then on your phone: MLB, any hitter's pick, the hit tracker's
season tab: xBA under his AVG, xSLG under his SLG, and a ball's tooltip
ends with its xBA; the note under the chart says what the test found.

**AE. Every league's charts open the same way** (step 73; nothing to run).

pitches, a basketball player's shots, a skater's shots, a goalie's saves;

**AF. Every league's Most Likely pick carries the same information**
(step 74; read-only, seconds). Nothing to run for the builds: each

cd /srv/qellys && for f in recommendations cfb mlb_recommendations nba wnba nhl; do python3 -c "import json,sys; f=sys.argv[1]; import os; p='data/built/'+f+'.json'; d=json.load(open(p if os.path.exists(p) else 'web/data/'+f+'.json')); ml=d.get('most_likely') or []; print(f, len(ml), 'picks |', sum(1 for r in ml if r.get('matchup_card')), 'matchup |', sum(1 for r in ml if r.get('mate_card')), 'teammate |', d.get('likely_board',{}).get('matchup_source'))" $f 2>/dev/null || echo "$f: no board"; done

The baseball card reads the season's stored plate appearances, which the

```

**AM. The site came up empty on the 14th** (step 81; read-only, about a
minute; paste all of it). Nothing to run for the fix itself: the box pulls
it, and this first restart happens at once (the app running now is too old
to say whether it is mid-sweep). Run this if the boards are still empty an
hour after A shows the fix, or whenever you are on the box, so we know what
the last two days actually did to it:
```
git -C /srv/qellys log --oneline -1; cat /srv/qellys/data/autoupdate.json; echo
systemctl show qellys -p ActiveState -p NRestarts -p ExecMainStartTimestamp -p MemoryCurrent
free -m; swapon --show; df -h /srv | tail -1
journalctl -k --since "2 days ago" --no-pager | grep -iE "oom-kill|out of memory|killed process" | tail -12
journalctl -u qellys --since "2 days ago" --no-pager | grep -iE "killed by signal|out of memory|database is locked|build failed|mlb charts|nba shots|charts:|watchdog|auto-update" | tail -30
python3 -c "import json,time; d=json.load(open('/srv/qellys/web/data/heartbeat.json')); print(round((time.time()-d['at_epoch'])/60), 'min since the last cycle;', d.get('swept'), '; cycle', d.get('cycle_s'), 's; running', d.get('commit'), ';', d.get('disk_free_gb'), 'GB free')"
for f in recommendations cfb mlb_recommendations nba wnba nhl ufc; do printf "%-20s %s\n" $f "$(stat -c %y /srv/qellys/web/data/$f.json 2>/dev/null | cut -c1-16)"; done
for u in "/api/players/search?q=allen&sport=nfl&scope=sport" "/api/recommendations" "/api/nba/recommendations"; do curl -s -o /dev/null -w "%{http_code} %{size_download}B %{time_total}s  $u\n" "http://127.0.0.1:8000$u"; done
ps -eo pid,ni,rss,etime,args --sort=-rss | head -6
```
How to read it:
* The kernel lines (`oom-kill`, `Killed process ... python3`) are the
  memory wall. Before the fix they would name a python child near a
  gigabyte, from late on the 13th on. None after the fix landed is the
  fix working.
* The qellys lines: `killed by signal 9 ... out of memory` is a chores
  child the kernel stopped; `mlb charts` and `nba shots` lines are the
  backfills, which still read 400 and 300 games a night, now eight at a
  time. Paste any `database is locked` or `build failed` line.
* The heartbeat should say a few minutes since the last cycle and `ran`,
  and every board's time should be within the last hour. Past an hour old
  the page takes that board's picks off (the stale bar at the top says
  so) until the next build lands.
* Search should answer `200` with a few hundred bytes in well under a
  second. A `502` or `000` is the app not answering.
* In `ps`, the web server (`launch.py --bind`) is the one at NI 0; builds
  and chores run at NI 10, and each of them now also marks itself first
  for the kernel to stop (oom_score_adj 500), so if memory runs out again
  a child goes, not the site.
* From the next push on, `autoupdate.json` can say `the restart waits for
  the board sweep in progress (N min in; waited M of 45 min)`: the new
  page is already served, and the box restarts into the new code when
  that sweep ends, 45 minutes at most. That is the new normal, not a
  fault; `cat /srv/qellys/data/cache/sweep.json` shows the sweep it is
  waiting on.

```
Paste the line back. `None None` for the position and number means his

```

**AP. The hit page's seven new pieces** (step 84; one read-only command,

cd /srv/qellys && sudo -u qellys python3 hitpagecheck.py

Paste it all back. How to read it:

6. `video`: reads "dropped 2026-10-14" (step 99): the page links no

7. `action photo`: `200 image/jpeg` is MLB's photo there. `not there:

A part that says `not there yet` is the app not yet restarted on this

```
Then on your phone open any MLB hitter's pick and his chart: his action

% beside hard hit, and the line "MLB's own · Baseball Savant · as of"

**AQ. Every league's Pick of the Day reaches its record** (steps 85 and
86; one read-only command, seconds). Nothing to run for either change:
from the first build on this code, each league's first qualifying pick of
the day is recorded, as the NFL's and MLB's always were, and the NBA and
NHL boards can name one off their game lines. Run this after the next
college, NBA or NHL build whose card says BET (college plays Thursday to
Saturday, the NHL most nights, the NBA from the 20th):
```
cd /srv/qellys && sudo -u qellys python3 potdcheck.py
```
Two lines a league. `journal:` is what the record holds (how many, how
many riding, W-L-P, the first and last day); `card:` is what the board
shows now. On a BET card the line should end `recorded today`. `NOT
RECORDED` on a card older than one build is the line I need. An NBA or
NHL pick reads like any other (`BET BOS ML at -130 DraftKings (sharp fair
58%)`); `NO BET — No pick today: no game line on this board` there now
means the build had no odds pull with Pinnacle's prices on it (a quiet
cycle, or before the odds were bought), and anything else after `NO BET`
is the bar the best line missed; `the price is older than the freshness
bar` (step 89) means the odds the build had were over six hours old, so
nothing was named until a fresh pull. The last line lists the leagues
record.json carries a Pick of the Day record for: every one that names a
pick (NFL, college, MLB, NBA, NHL), empty or not; never UFC, which names
none. Then on your phone, Record → each of those leagues: the Pick of the
Day section is there, reading "Nothing settled yet." until its first pick
grades; UFC's page has none.

`savant_arsenal_batter_2026_alt.csv` is the pitch-type board in use: a few
thousand rows, fetched within the last six hours (before this it never
changed after its first download). `savant_arsenal_batter_2026.csv` with 0
rows and an old date is the empty form from September, asked for only
when the others fail; leave it. The barrels and expected files should be
under six hours old too, and the two ending `_all` (the hit page's copies,
fetched nightly) under a day. A file still dated weeks back, or `FETCH
FAILED`, is the line I need: paste it. After the next MLB build,
`grep -E "Pitch mix" data/logs/mlb_build.log | tail -1` should say
`arsenal board: N hitter(s) (2026)` with no error after it.

**AS. The Status page tells the truth after a deploy** (step 88; one

Status: the line at the top should read "Everything is running and every

cd /srv/qellys && sudo -u qellys python3 launch.py --boards

`typical refresh cycle` is the box's sweep in seconds (about 1,500 to

`cat /srv/qellys/data/autoupdate.json` says `no restart`; any other push
restarts as before.

**AT. Past UFC voids that look like the midnight bug** (step 90;
read-only, about a minute: it asks ESPN about each voided fight). Paste
all of it:
```
cd /srv/qellys && sudo -u qellys python3 ufcvoids.py
```
Every UFC pick the ledger voided, in two groups. NO WINNER is how the bug

runs the page counts, how many were out of every park and how many of half
or fewer, the balls that were not homers but were gone somewhere, and his
first five homers with their count of parks; `fences None` means his balls
in play carry no distance yet. The second, during a live MLB game, prints
each game file's pitcher, the batter's side, and whether the next-pitch
table is in it and how many of his pitches it rests on; `next_pitch False`
for a pitcher means none of his pitches are stored yet (the hit page's
nightly fill writes them):
```
curl -s "http://127.0.0.1:8000/api/players/chart?kind=spray&sport=mlb&player=Aaron%20Judge" | python3 -c "import json,sys; d=json.load(sys.stdin); f=d.get('fences'); print('fences', None if f is None else ('HR', len(f['hr']), 'no-doubt', f['no_doubt'], 'doubters', f['doubters'], 'gone somewhere', f['near_n'])); print([(r['date'], r['n'], r.get('d')) for r in ((f or {}).get('hr') or [])[:5]])"
cd /srv/qellys && sudo -u qellys python3 -c "
import json, glob
for p in sorted(glob.glob('web/data/pbp/mlb_*.json')):
    d=json.load(open(p)); n=d.get('next_pitch'); c=d.get('current') or {}
    print(p.split('/')[-1], (d.get('live') or {}).get('state'), c.get('pitcher'), c.get('bat_side'), 'next_pitch', bool(n), n and n.get('n'))
"
```

**BR. Every corner of the UFC card carries his record** (step 123; read-only,
a second, after the next UFC build on the new code). It prints how many fights
the card shows, how many corners carry a path, then the first six corners
with their stored fights, wins and losses by how they ended. A corner with
`None` has no fights on file yet (the scheduler's UFC tick reads a few
fighters at a time). It reads the subscribers' copy in `data/built/`: the
fight reads are behind the paywall, so the public `web/data/ufc.json` always
shows `0 fights` (the first version of this check read that one):
```
cd /srv/qellys && sudo -u qellys python3 -c "
import json; d=json.load(open('data/built/ufc.json'))
rows=(d.get('picks') or [])+(d.get('pass_list') or [])
fs=[f for r in rows for f in r.get('fighters') or []]

for f in fs[:6]:

"
```

```
curl -s "http://127.0.0.1:8000/api/feed/pickem?sport=nfl" | python3 -c "import sys, json; d = json.load(sys.stdin); print(d['label'], len(d['games']), 'games,', sum(g['locked'] for g in d['games']), 'begun')"
cd /srv/qellys && sudo -u qellys nice -n 19 python3 - <<'EOF'
import sqlite3
from engine.nhl import xg as X
m = X.load()
print('model: league rate', m and m.get('league'), 'fit on', m and m.get('n'), 'attempts')
c = sqlite3.connect('file:data/history.db?mode=ro', uri=True); c.row_factory = sqlite3.Row
tot = {}
for r in c.execute("SELECT season, kind, empty_net, is_goal, goalie, dist, angle, shot_type, rebound, rush, strength FROM nhl_shots"):
    if r['empty_net'] or not m: continue
    s = tot.setdefault(r['season'], [0, 0.0, 0.0, 0, 0])
    x = X.xg(m, dict(r))
    s[0] += r['is_goal']
    if r['kind'] in ('goal', 'sog'): s[1] += x
    if r['kind'] in X.UNBLOCKED: s[2] += x
    if r['kind'] == 'miss': s[3] += 1; s[4] += bool(r['goalie'])
for se, (g, on, allx, mi, mg) in sorted(tot.items()):
    print(se, 'goals', g, '| xG on net', round(on), '| xG every unblocked attempt', round(allx), '| misses', mi, 'with a goalie', mg)
EOF

   the bar's count.

   season held out in turn, k (the share of the absent man's minutes his
   teammates take) fitted on the others, and it HOLDS only if minutes and
   points both get closer in every held-out season. Run the first line

pulling its goalie late, or the empty-netter that follows, and that is what

minute by minute, fitted from every regulation goal on the clock, and moves
nothing until it beats the old model on seasons it never saw. The first
line stores each goal's clock (it reads the saved play files first, so it

second fits, tests and saves; paste its last ten lines. The third says

**CO. The price rule reaches posted picks** (step 146).

Your CN paste read 247 board picks, 0 at the price. The cause:

- A pick counts as posted once it has been on a previous build, and that
  is nearly every pick by Saturday evening.
- The first cut skipped posted picks.
- The board also takes a posted pick's number back from the list on every
  build. So the rule could touch a pick on its first build only, then
  lose it.

The fix:

- A posted pick keeps its line, price and book as before. Its chance is
  today's on every build, and the price now judges that chance too.
- The list shows the same number (your one-bet-one-chance rule).
- The board now carries its own counts: picks checked, picks with no book
  quote, picks with under three books at the line, picks with a field,
  picks above it, and picks moved. A zero now says why it is zero.

Run this after the box has pulled and the next NFL build has run (about
half an hour). The first line should name `The price rule reaches posted
picks`, and the file time should be later than that commit:

```
cd /srv/qellys && git log -1 --format='%h %ci %s' && date -u -r data/built/recommendations.json && sudo -u qellys python3 -c "import json;d=json.load(open('data/built/recommendations.json'));b=d.get('likely_board') or {};print('price trust:',b.get('price_trust','(none: this board was built by older code)'));rows=(b.get('rows') or [])+(b.get('held') or []);t=[r for r in rows if 'de-vigged price says' in (r.get('check_notes') or {}).get('model','')];print(len(rows),'board picks,',len(t),'shown at the price');[print(' ',r.get('player'),r.get('market'),r.get('side'),r.get('line'),r.get('trust_from'),'->',r.get('model_prob')) for r in t[:8]]"
```

**CQ. The game report on every NFL game page** (step 148).
Your "I want a full layed out detailed explanation for every nfl game just
like how the report is. Obv keep out the prop picks". Every NFL game page
now opens on a written report, in the research reports' order:

- The short version.
- Game facts, then the market: the line and what it implies, where it
  opened, the prediction markets and their money, and the simulator.
- How the units rank, both offenses against the other defense.
- How each team plays: run rate, play action, the target tree, the
  coverage and the blitz.
- Injuries and what they open.
- Who's guarding who: a verdict on every key player, the soft corner, and
  where each defense leaks and holds.
- The trenches, the red zone, coaching and the schedule.
- How the game could go, players to watch, weather.
- What to confirm before kickoff.

Every number is the board's own. A section with nothing behind it is left
out, and no line, price or chance on a player appears anywhere in it.
With the report on the page, the matchup scan and the game plan's
narrative steps step aside, so nothing is said twice. The replay chart
moves inside the report. The game plan keeps its plays. The report is
paid; a reader without the subscription sees what it holds and the
usual matchup scan.

It does not slow the site down. The report's page code loads only when
a game page opens, the way the Toolbox does, and if it ever fails to
load, the page shows the old matchup scan and full game plan instead. The
wiring that has to stay in the main file is 0.6 KB, so its size limit
went from 496 to 497 KB (the reason is written in the test).

The Paris game was read as a dome. nflverse gives a game abroad the home
team's stadium until it is played. Stade de France (Steelers at Saints,
Sunday 9:30 AM ET) read "dome" because the Superdome is one, so no
forecast was pulled and the report said "a fixed dome". Open-air grounds
abroad (London, Paris, Munich, Berlin, Mexico City, São Paulo, Rio,
Dublin, Melbourne) now read outdoors and get their own city's forecast.
A stadium whose roof can close, like Madrid or Frankfurt, keeps the
schedule's word. The report also leaves out the surface for a game
abroad, because that is the home team's too.

**CR. Everything in the report, a report for every league, what changed,
copy and share** (step 149). Your "I want you too do all of it besides
D": A, B and C are built. D (the public betting percentages, the referee
before kickoff, PFF grades) waits until you have read this.

- **A, the NFL report reads the history.** Under every key player's card:
  his season, his last three games, a trend when he is running hot or
  cold, and his red-zone work. The practice report: how each listed man
  practised and how often players listed that way have played. The role
  changes the detector sees, as a breakout watch. When the two teams last
  met, with the lines they closed at. Each team's turnover margin and
  tempo. The kicking air: the air's density and the wind along the field.
  The coaches' calls: how often each goes for it on fourth and short, two
  point tries, how often it throws two scores up and two scores down, and
  its pass rate over what the situation calls for.
- **B, a report for every league.** College games get the same report in
  college's terms (short names, the AP rank, a neutral site, a conference
  game, college's own unit stats). MLB: the pitching matchup as a table,
  each lineup against tonight's hand, the pens, the park, the weather and
  the plate umpire, and a read on every hitter and starter. NHL: the tale
  of the tape with ranks and tonight's expected goals, the goalies
  (confirmed or not, save rate, goals saved above expected), the injury
  report. NBA and WNBA: rest and travel, who carries each team over his
  last ten, where each defence gives up the most and the fewest, the
  injury report. The run line and the puck line are said by name, and no
  team totals are read off them.
- **C.** Ask Qellys reads the report, for members. Every report keeps a
  **What changed** log under the short version: the line moving half a
  point, a man ruled out, the forecast turning, a starter scratched, a
  goalie confirmed, the lineups out, each stamped Eastern time. Small
  wobbles are not written; a slow drift is, once it adds up. Two buttons
  over every report: **Copy the report** (the whole thing as text) and
  **Share this game** (only the game, kickoff, line and link, so nothing
  paid goes out in a forwarded message).

**CS. The NBA, ready, with what the NFL has** (step 150). Your "make sure
nba is ready to go ... match nfl for like all features ... offense vs
defense ... matchup details ... key matchups and breakout players":

- **Already working, checked:** NBA Edge picks and Most Likely picks are
  posted and recorded every build: the Edge picks staked, Most Likely and
  the one board on paper, the Pick of the Day in its own book, all settled
  from the box scores the nightly job stores.
- **Offense against defense.** Every NBA team's scoring and what it
  allows, its offense and defense per 100 possessions, pace, shooting (the
  effective field-goal rate both ways), the threes both ways, the
  offensive glass and the turnovers both ways. Each comes with its rank of
  30, and the points tonight's lines expect. Last season's numbers carry
  each team until this season's games take over: this season counts
  games / (games + 10) of the blend.
- **Key matchups.** Up to five per game: one side's offense near the top
  against the other's defense near the bottom (or the reverse), widest
  gap first, and the pace when both teams play fast or both play slow.
- **A read on every key player**, the NFL's could shine / could struggle
  / breakout. It counts the defense in his own stats, the points his team
  is expected to score, his minutes (and his share of the plays) over his
  last five, what the logs show when tonight's ruled-out teammate sat,
  rest on either side, and the blowout. A breakout spot needs a role
  change (minutes, usage or a teammate out) behind a strong case.
- **The reads lean Most Likely** and decide the one board's matchup check,
  as in football and baseball. **Matchup picks** per game (his stats on
  the read's side where our number agrees, at a real sportsbook price, a
  20-minute role at least) are on paper under matchup_prop, and settle
  like the rest.
- **On the page:** every NBA game has a **Matchup scan** section (the tape,
  the key matchups, the reads). For members the game report carries all
  of it: **The matchup** (tale of the tape plus key matchups) and
  **Players to watch** (breakout watch, could shine, could struggle). Ask

**CT. NBA opening night: no lines, no live, a stale Pick of the Day**
(step 151). Your 7:04 PM screenshot: the 76ers @ Knicks card read "lines
post closer to tip-off", Top Picks was empty and the Pick of the Day read
"decided 04:17 UTC, when 0 of the slate had not started". You also said the
games weren't showing live. Three causes found in the code, all fixed:

- **The NBA build was cut off after three minutes.** The launcher kills a
  build that runs past 180 seconds unless that build gets a longer limit.
  MLB, the NFL and college got 600 seconds. The NBA, WNBA and NHL never
  did. Tonight is the first night the NBA board priced real props. A build
  killed every cycle leaves the board file at the last build that finished:
  no lines, no props, and live scores frozen at that build. All three now
  get 600 seconds.
- **The NBA had no cheap way to buy game lines.** Its only paid pull was
  the props pull, 19 credits a game, on the NBA's small share of the day.
  A night the pacer declined it had no moneyline, spread or total at all.
  The NBA and WNBA now have the NFL's and college's 3-credit tier: the
  whole slate's moneyline, spread and total in one request, on its own
  clock. It is bought only for games that haven't started, and every build
  reads the saved copy for free.
- **The Pick of the Day kept a "no bet" from Monday night.** At 12:17 AM
  Eastern the board was still on Monday's empty slate (the slate rolls at
  5 AM). That decline saw zero games, and the tie rule kept it all day.
  Now a decline that saw nothing never holds, and a card from a different
  slate date is never carried.

**CU. The live scoreboard on a phone: logos over the score** (step 152).
Your 8:41 PM screenshot of 76ers @ Knicks at the half: the 76ers logo sat
under the "54" and the Knicks logo ran off the edge of the card. Two causes,

  wide as all three buttons: 260px of a 340px card on a phone. Each team
  was left 32px for a 56px logo and the score. The buttons are now their
  own row under the scoreboard.

  logo is now 36px, and it gets smaller before it can touch the score.

**CV. The month's odds plan: 100k credits, NFL first** (step 153). Your
"we have 100k credit per month for figure out how too budget in all the
sports that are currently active while keeping NFL at number one
priority". The pacer now runs on a monthly plan (docs/ODDS_PLAN.md has
all of it):

- **NFL funded first, in full.** Then college. Then the NBA, NHL, MLB and
  UFC split what's left at the same percentage, so a short month trims
  each of them a little instead of shutting the last one off. Extra room
  goes to the NFL first, but only after every league's normal cadence is
  paid for.
- **Every league gets a daily ceiling** sized to its real cadence: NBA and
  NHL three prop pulls a game day (noon, the pre-game run, 6pm ET), college
  eight on Saturday, the NFL its full Sunday. Before this, the NBA got
  about 2% of a weekday, which was one pull. That's the opening-night board
  with no lines.
- **November at 100,000:** NFL 42,020 and college 13,940, both in full. NBA
  16,667, NHL 9,001, MLB 426 and UFC 445, each at 86% of its target. Held
  back: 12,000 for closing lines, 5,000 buffer, 500 reserve.
- **Cheaper with it:** game lines no longer refresh every 15 minutes on days
  with no game within 36 hours (NFL Tuesdays and Fridays were about 290
  credits each). The live line polls every 10 minutes instead of 5. UFC is
  metered at its real one credit a fight.

**CW. Fantasy reads the NFL board: this week's projection on every desk**
(step 154). Your "make sure we are using that data correctly for fantasy
... make sure we wrap that into it how it should be". Until now every
league desk valued a player at his season average, with no matchup,
script, weather or injuries, and a player on a bye at his average too.
Now (docs/FANTASY_WEEK.md has all of it):

- **The NFL build writes this week in fantasy points** beside the board,
  members only. Each stat is the board's own projection (the defence
  against his position, the game script, the weather, his quarterback or
  a teammate out). Touchdowns come from the touchdown model for every
  skill player. A questionable player counts at the measured chance he
  plays (64.6%; 51.8% after a week of no practice). A bye or an Out is
  zero.
- **Your league desk** scores the lineup and the head-to-head on it, under
  your league's scoring. Byes and Outs are zero, and so are Sleeper's IR
  and suspended players. Trades stay on season averages, because a trade
  is the rest of the season. "Best pickups this week" lists the best of
  the week that nobody in your league has.
- **The Fantasy page** opens on a "This week" room: every player by
  position, with the matchup, the chance he plays, where eight weeks in
  ten land, his touchdown chance and why. The player panel shows his week.
  It also fixes a bug: the full profile used to drop the start/sit call a
  moment after opening.
- **Ask** answers start/sit from the same numbers.
- **Measured on 2023-2025:** for every two players at a position, it had
  the higher scorer ahead 72.3% of the time, against 71.3% for the season
  average. It missed by 4.66 points a week against 4.83. Byes and Outs
  aren't in that number.

**DA. Your new data in Most Likely and Edge** (step 158). Your "Are all of
our new data and changes being pushed to the most likely bets too bc it

- 25 measured inputs move the projection itself, so both boards; 7 more

  fantasy week).
- 6 are notes on purpose: they measured no lift (charting, Next Gen Stats).

The gap was the 11 inventions: the practice report, kicker air, rookie
priors, role changes, simulated parlays, book menus, book recipes,
attention, book copies, honest ranges, the NHL endgame. Each joins Most
Likely and Edge on its own once its saved verdict holds. But the verdicts
were saved by hand once (BX to CE, Oct 17), a third of a season in, and
never again. Now the box measures them again every Thursday (the daily
chores start `engine/inventionfit`, one at a time, about half an hour,
no odds credits), and one that holds joins with the next build.

This Thursday's chores ran before the code landed, so the first automatic
run is next Thursday. To have fresh verdicts for Sunday, run it once
yourself, not during a game (about half an hour, low priority):

```

```

**DB. Why Luther Burden III isn't on our boards** (step 159). Your "every
report I have recommended Luther burden III but we don't have him once in

ranks receivers with. On the box's curve we rate him BELOW the books:

- Edge shows only bets where we think the book is wrong in our favour. On
  Burden we rate him at or under the books: 4+ catches is about 66% to us
  and about −260 (72% before the vig) at the book; 40+ yards is 59% to 67%
  to us (the box narrows the curve for a steady receiver like him, by an
  amount its own weekly fit sets; DC prints it) and about −260. And
  receiving yards has been off Edge entirely since August, when our model
  couldn't beat the closing lines there (engine/calibrate, SHUT_MARKETS).
- Most Likely shows the likeliest bets at 55% or better, priced −250 or
  shorter, and at most the top 40. 4+ catches and 40+ yards are about
  −260, just past your −250 cap. His cheaper lines are mostly under the
  55% floor to us: 5+ catches 47%, 56+ yards 38% to 41%; 45+ yards (52%
  to 58%) is right at it.

**DD. Is our receiving curve too cautious on receivers like Burden?**
(step 161). No, so nothing changes. I rebuilt 2021 through this week from
the same public data the box stores, refitted the curve the way the box
does (the fit came out the same as the box's), and held out each season
in turn. Then, by how many yards each receiver was projected, I compared
what the curve claimed for bets like Burden's (over about three quarters
of his projection) with what actually happened:

- Receivers projected 45–60 yards: the curve said 58–59%; they landed
  55–62%, depending on how much you trust a hot streak.
- Projected 4–5.5 catches: the curve said 62–64%; they landed 59–64%.
- The plain curve behind the 72% I first gave you said 66–69% for the
  same bets. It was 10 points too high.

So the board's lower number on Burden was the honest one, and history
says the books' −260 (69% after the vig) was a steep price for a bet like
that. He hit a 60-something-percent bet; one game can't say more than
that. If anything
our curve is a bit narrow, not too cautious: real games have more duds
and more big days than it allows, and fixing that would lower these
bets, not raise them. I didn't change it: the box fits it on recent-game
averages but prices off the full projection, so a width tuned here could
make the board worse.

It changes nothing; to see it yourself on the box (a few minutes, any
time, read-only):

```

```

**DE. The bigger lines on every Most Likely pick** (step 162). Your "If we
know a player can do well we should be recommending the higher line bc
obv it would have hit." You chose to keep each pick the likeliest line
and show the bigger ones under it:

- Every player pick on Most Likely has a "Bigger lines" fold under it,
  closed so the phone card stays the same size. Open it for up to three
  bigger lines further out on the same side (50+, 60+, 70+ above a 40+
  pick; lower lines under an under). Each shows its best price across the
  books, our chance, and the price it's worth betting at.
- A line whose price beats our chance by 2% or more (the Pick of the
  Day's own bar) is marked "priced better than its chance" and is always
  listed, even past the first three.
- Left off: long shots (under 20%), lines where our chance and the book's
  own price are more than 10 points apart, and books you can't bet.
- They are never picks: not staked, not journaled. The pick and its
  record don't change. Every sport's board gets them wherever books post
  alternate lines.

Why the pick stays the low line, for the record. Receivers projected
50–60 yards since 2021 (1,080 games) went 40+ in 64% of them, 50+ in
52%, 60+ in 42%, 70+ in 32% and 80+ in 26%. A higher line pays more
because it misses more. It's the better bet only when its price beats its
chance, which is what the mark says.

for r in rows[:5]:
    print(' ', r.get('player'), r.get('side'), r.get('line'), r.get('odds'), '->', ', '.join(f\"{c['line']} {c['odds']} {round(100 * c['prob'])}%{' *' if c.get('better') else ''}\" for c in r['bigger_lines']))

**DF. The iPhone Duo** (step 163). Your "Make app work seamlessly with
iPhone duo ... The big screen we can do a desktop sort of view. Also make
it work when the duo is half open." No box commands: it's all in the page,
and the box picks it up on its own.

  there (its own buttons take a column down the right), so it's short:
  on Most Likely the first pick used to start under the tab bar. On short
  phone screens the decorative stadium band now steps aside and the
  league circles shrink, so a pick shows when the page opens.
- Open, either way you hold it: the desktop view. Sidebar beside the page,
  no hamburger, no tab bar, and the header stays put instead of sliding
  away. On its side Safari gives about 951 x 543; upright about 669 x 825.
- Folding and unfolding: nothing reloads. You stay on the same pick (the
  card at the top of the screen is put back where it was), and an open
  menu closes when the screen opens.
- Half open: Safari tells websites nothing about the fold (no event, no
  setting). One test found Safari moves to the front screen when
  half-folded, which gives you the phone layout at the same spot. If it
  stays on the big screen, the page is the same size as flat open, so the
  desktop view applies.
- Unchanged: phones, phones on their side, iPads and desktops. One fix
  rode along: on screens 900 to 1000 wide the header ran 29px off the
  right edge; the date range now steps aside there.

The sizes above come from Apple's simulator, not a real Duo. Once the box
has the code, on your Duo: folded, open the site (phone layout, tab bar
at the bottom), scroll to a pick, unfold (sidebar on the left, the same
pick at the top), turn it upright (still the sidebar), fold again (phone
layout, same spot). If any step shows the wrong layout, send a screenshot
of it and say how you were holding it.

**DG. Are our carry projections right?** (step 164). Your "yes do the
carry projection check", after Swift carried 22 times on our 15. I replayed
every running back's game in 2025 and 2026 from week 3 (1,625 games), each
projected only from the weeks before it, the way the board builds it:

- No bias to fix. Lead backs land 0.4 carries under projection on average,
  inside the noise, and the projection beats a plain average of a back's
  last three games (3.3 carries off a game against 3.4).
- Nothing left in the spread: lead backs on favourites and underdogs land
  where we put them, so the game-script step is the right size.
- The misses are game flow. A lead back whose team loses by a score or
  more gets 3.4 fewer carries; one whose team wins big, 1.2 more. Swift's
  22 (Bears won by 6) is that, and no number made before kickoff can know.
- The biggest misses are backups starting for a hurt lead back. The live
  board's injury step sees those; the replay can't.

`--seasons 2025-2026` for both, about a quarter of an hour):

their 70%+ bets priced −251 to −350 under Most Likely, labelled and never

D, built:

- **The referee before kickoff.** Every NFL game report's "Before
  kickoff" names the referee once a source has him: named by hand first,
  then ESPN's game summary, then Football Zebras' weekly assignments
  post (credited). Beside the name are his games since 2024 and the
  league's: penalties a game (the league runs 12.9), how often his games
  went over the closing total, how often home teams covered. The line
  ends with what we measured on 7,340 games: a referee's record adds
  nothing the closing line doesn't already have, so it's context. The
  numbers refit every Tuesday. The first fit is the first command below,
  so tomorrow's reports have them.
- **Grades.** PFF's terms limit its grades to personal, non-commercial
  use, so a page members pay for can't show them; using them would need
  a licence from PFF. Instead, each key player's card in the report has
  **Our grade**: 0–100 against his position this season, from the free
  Next Gen Stats and PFR charting the site already reads. Receivers:
  yards a target, separation, yards after the catch over expected,
  drops. Backs: yards over expected, after contact, broken tackles. QBs:
  completion rate over expected, yards an attempt, bad throws,
  interceptions. Each corner gets a coverage grade. Every part is shown
  with its rank, and the report says once that the grade is ours and
  part of no number. Luther Burden III, through week 6: 68, against 72
  wide receivers.
- **Public betting percentages.** The pages that print these (Action
  Network, VSiN, the books' own) don't offer them for reuse. SharpAPI
  sells them: DraftKings' and Circa's share of the bets and of the money,
  every five minutes. Its docs disagree on whether splits come with the
  free plan or need Pro ($229 a month). Built and **off until you set a
  key**: once set, the report's market section says, for example, "72%
  of the bets and 58% of the money on KC to cover", with Circa's money
  beside it, as context the line already prices. It's paid-only, and
  every reading is kept so fading the public can be measured on our own
  record. To try a free key (sign up at sharpapi.io; the key is typed at
  the prompt, never pasted into a file):

```
cd /srv/qellys && sudo ./deploy/setenv.sh SHARPAPI_KEY
cd /srv/qellys && sudo -u qellys env $(sudo grep -E '^SHARPAPI_KEY=' /etc/qellys/env | xargs) python3 -m engine.splits --probe
```

reply's shape; if the reader misses a field, the fix is one line.

**DJ. Your DI paste, read** (step 167). The referees' numbers, the update
check, the inventions' labels, the grades and the past-cap lists all came
back as they should. Four things didn't:

- **The −9900 line was still there.** Savion Williams's under 19.5 is a
  posted pick, and a posted pick is last build's row carried forward,
  which kept last build's bigger lines. When today's ladder had no line
  worth listing, nothing replaced them. Every build now clears them
  first, so a posted pick shows today's lines or none.
- **161 marks were left.** Nearly every alternate rung the books post is
  over-only, so those rungs had no market number to be held to, and our
  raw chance stood. An over-only rung is now held to its own book's
  price, de-vigged the way the site de-vigs every one-sided price. A mark
  now means a book's price beats the market's chance at that number.
  After the next NFL build, DE should show only a handful.
- **No referee was named, 0 of 14.** Either ESPN doesn't list officials
  before kickoff, or Football Zebras' feed didn't return the week's post,
  or its rows didn't read. The first command says which (read-only):

```

  Until a source works, `--set "AWAY@HOME=Name"` names one by hand (DI).
- **SharpAPI said 403.** Python's default client name is often refused
  by an API's firewall before the key is read, and the probe threw away
  the reply. The client now gives its own name, and a refusal prints what
  SharpAPI said. Run the probe again:

```
cd /srv/qellys && sudo -u qellys env $(sudo grep -E '^SHARPAPI_KEY=' /etc/qellys/env | xargs) python3 -m engine.splits --probe
```

  systemctl restart qellys`). If it names the plan, splits aren't in the
  free plan, and it's your call whether Pro is worth it. If it names the
  key, check it on SharpAPI's dashboard and set it again.

**DK. Your DJ paste, read** (step 168).

- **SharpAPI: "This endpoint requires Pro tier or higher."** The key
  works; the free plan doesn't include splits. Pro is $229 a month on
  their pricing page. Your call. Until then the builds ask at most once
  a day, since a refusal is remembered for 24 hours. If you upgrade, run
  the DJ probe once (it always asks, and clears the memory), then `sudo
  systemctl restart qellys`. If you'd rather it never ask:
  `sudo ./deploy/setenv.sh --unset SHARPAPI_KEY`.
- **Referees.** ESPN lists no officials before kickoff (its summary had
  only venue and weather). Football Zebras' week 7 post was found, but
  it's written as sentences ("Alan Eck will be in Chicago on Thursday as
  the Patriots square off with the Bears"), not a table. The reader now
  splits each sentence at every referee it names. A part becomes a game
  when every team it names, by nickname, full name or a one-team city,
  plays in the same game this week; a team plays once a week, so the
  order doesn't matter. A passing mention ("the game Bill Vinovich worked
  in Dallas") loses to the referee who names both teams. A tie, or one
  referee read onto two games, names nobody. Once the box has this, the
  first line prints every sentence and what it became; the second lists
  the week:

```
cd /srv/qellys && sudo -u qellys python3 -m engine.nflrefs --why | tail -30
cd /srv/qellys && sudo -u qellys python3 -m engine.nflrefs
```

  Tomorrow's reports carry them from the next NFL build on. Any game
  still `-` can be named by hand (`--set`, DI).
- **DE still showed the −9900 and 136 marks.** The board in that run was
  built before the fix reached the box: an NFL build takes 10+ minutes,
  and the check ran minutes after the push. Its own stamp settles it:
  `built` should be after the update's `at`, the marks should be a
  handful, and nothing should be priced past −1000:

```
cd /srv/qellys && cat data/autoupdate.json; echo; sudo -u qellys python3 -c "
import json
d = json.load(open('data/built/recommendations.json'))
rows = [r for r in d.get('most_likely') or [] if r.get('bigger_lines')]
print('built', d.get('built_at'), '·', len(rows), 'picks with bigger lines,', sum(1 for r in rows for c in r['bigger_lines'] if c.get('better')), 'marked,', sum(1 for r in rows for c in r['bigger_lines'] if (c.get('odds') or 0) < -1000), 'priced past -1000')
"
```

- **NBA and NHL: 0 props priced, so no Most Likely.** That's the odds
  plan (CV): after the NFL's 11,260 credits and college's 4,996, about
  960 are left for every other league until the plan renews on Nov 1.
  NBA props for the rest of the month need about 5,200, and NHL's about
  3,000. Moving credits from college, or buying more, is your call;
  otherwise they come back on the 1st.

**DL. Every fold and flip, built for each phone; the bug scan; the app
stores** (step 169). Your "make it for any Galaxy Flip or Fold ... every
single form factor", "make it unique for that phone", "optimize it for
the App Store and the Google Play Store" and "we need a debugging thing to
scan for bugs".

Every fold and flip. Measured first: 42 screen shapes × 10 pages, from the
first Galaxy Fold's 280px cover to a 1920 desktop. Nothing ran off the
screen; the work was making each shape feel built for, the way the Duo's
own apps do (Notes and Messages in two columns when open, MLB's game above
the stats when half folded):

- **Fold, Pixel Fold and OnePlus Open inner screens** get the open Duo's
  desktop view (the sidebar beside the page) instead of the phone's.
- **List beside detail** on a wide open screen held in your hand (the Duo
  on its side, a Surface Duo, a TriFold, a tablet on its side): tap a
  pick and it opens on the right while Most Likely stays on the left; tap
  another and the right side changes; fold the phone and the pick fills
  the screen.
- **Folded like a book**, the two sides split exactly at the hinge.
- **Flex Mode**, folded like a laptop (a Flip on the table): the page stays
  in the top half and the bottom half is big buttons.
- **Flip and Razr cover screens:** slim bars and icons only, picks first.
  **The narrowest covers** show each pick on two rows, so a name is never
  one word per line.
- The installed app is no longer locked to portrait.

The hinge comes from the standard fold APIs: Chrome and Edge have them on
Galaxy and Pixel foldables. On your Duo, Safari 27.1 has them behind two
switches. To try the half-folded layouts there, turn on both "Device
Posture API" and "Viewport Segments API" in Settings → Apps → Safari →
Advanced → Feature Flags. Without them, the Duo still gets the open and
folded layouts by shape. On the phone: open Most Likely on the Duo turned
sideways, tap a pick, then fold it.

The bug scan. A crash on anyone's screen used to show them an apology and
tell nobody else. Now each one reaches the box: the message, where in the
code, the page, the screen size and the browser family. No IP address,
no account; the privacy policy says so in 2.6. One command reads it
together with each build's warnings and tracebacks, any board older than
3 hours, the self-update and the site's errors. The daily pass runs it
and **mails you only when something is broken** (the address that gets
the service-failure mail). Read-only, any time:

```
cd /srv/qellys && sudo -u qellys python3 -m engine.bugscan
cd /srv/qellys && sudo -u qellys python3 -m engine.bugscan --hours 168 | head -60
```

The app stores, in docs/APP_STORES.md (updated). Done: every screen size
and orientation (Play now requires apps to target Android 16, which ignores
a portrait lock on foldables and tablets anyway), account deletion in the
app plus a public page for Play's form
(`https://qellysbook.com/privacy.html#delete-account`), crash reports
declared, and a screenshot tool for every size each store asks for. It
runs on your laptop against the live site:

```
npm i -g playwright && npx playwright install chromium
NODE_PATH=$(npm root -g) node tools/store_screenshots.cjs https://qellysbook.com nfl
```

Yours to decide (also in that file): the **Bet it** links in a Play
build. Google's gambling policy generally doesn't allow apps outside its
licensed programme to facilitate wagering, and those buttons open a
sportsbook's bet slip filled in. Either the app build drops them (the
website keeps them) or you apply to the programme, which needs licences.
And for iOS: Apple's 4.2 rejects a website in a wrapper, so it needs a
native feature first (push alerts when a pick settles is the strongest).
US iOS apps may now link to the Stripe checkout (since May 2025).

**DM. Both store calls built; push alerts; a first screen that holds still;

just need it to work with the app store", "make sure we can use logos and
headshots", "more professional and more bug checks", the Duo's "liquid
glass", and "make sure it's going to be sharp and running quickly".
Everything is in docs/APP_STORES.md (top section and the checklist table)
and docs/IDEAS.md (the ranked list at the top).

- **The store app.** In the Play build (and the iOS one, when there is one)
  the site leaves out the Bet it and Tail it sportsbook sheets, every league
  logo and player photo (our drawn helmets, caps and initials instead), the
  meme-coin radar and the prediction markets; the Play build sells nothing
  until you enrol in Play's US external-links programme. The website keeps
  everything. Logos and photos in an app need a licence; the website still
  hotlinks ESPN's, which is your call.
- **Push alerts:** "This week's NFL picks are up — 6 picks on the board",
  "Last night: 8–3–1, +3.6u". Counts and the record, never a pick, nothing
  overnight. Works in browsers and the Play app as soon as this deploys; the
  box makes its own key. The iPhone app needs Apple's key
  (deploy/ios/README.md).
- **Speed:** on a slowed-down phone the page used to jump 0.78 of a screen
  while loading and freeze for 765 ms; now 0.02 and about 370 ms. The logo
  swelled to four times its width on every refresh (fixed), and the
  Toolbox ran a 2,000-run simulation in the background on every load
  (now only when you open it).
- **The Duo:** a floating glass sidebar, a glass bar, glass cards and
  capsule buttons when it's open; a glass tab bar on every phone. No blur
  (the thing that crashed your iPhone).
- The app opens on the league you were last on, or the one in season, not
  on baseball all winter. A screenshot of a slip shared from Photos opens
  Ask and grades it (Android). The installed app's icon shows your open bets.
  Crash reports now name the right line of code.

**DS. The stadiums come alive** (step 176). Your "keep going with any more
ideas like that or any other ideas in general to keep adding life to the
app".

- **The football field's big board is lit too:** the centre-hung board over
  the field photographs (NFL and college) shows the away team on its left
  screen and the home team on its right, each with a stripe of its colour
  and its score, and a football under the side that has the ball. Under our
  crown it says the game: "Q3 4:12 · 3RD & 8", OT, HALFTIME, END OF 3RD,
  FINAL, or the kickoff time.
- **Big moments go up on the boards:** TOUCHDOWN, FIELD GOAL, SAFETY,
  INTERCEPTION or FUMBLE on the football board; HOME RUN or GRAND SLAM on
  the ballpark's. A touchdown or a home run sets off fireworks over the
  stands, one burst in the scoring team's colour. Only a play that lands
  while you are watching counts, not one from while you were away, and a
  phone set to reduce motion gets the word without the fireworks.
- **The wind over the ballpark:** blowing out or in at 5 mph or more, with
  the roof open, thin streaks run over the outfield in its direction, more
  of them and faster the harder it blows. A crosswind is not drawn (our
  data does not say which side it comes from), nor football's (a compass
  bearing, and the photograph has no north).
- **A game's last hour counts down:** its chip in the strip along the top
  of every live page reads "in 12:34" and ticks every second; on its own
  live page both boards count down too ("FIRST PITCH IN", "KICKOFF IN").

**DW. The live pages, caught up with the game** (step 180). Your "about
a 45-second delay between what actually happens on the TV compared to
... our app", "the play-by-play, the score, all of it is very delayed".

- **Why it was late.** Measured on the live site that afternoon: the box
  rewrote each game's file every 16.5 seconds (it slept 12, then worked
  4.5), but the score and clock under it were kept for 20 seconds, so
  they only moved on every other write, about every 33 seconds. Then the
  live game page asked for news every 12 seconds, and the Live tab
  answered from a copy kept 30 seconds and only asked again every 30.
  Added up, our own part could reach most of a minute.
- **On the box.** The score loops (NFL, college, NBA, WNBA, NHL and MLB)
  now sleep 5 seconds between passes while a game is on, and every cache
  under them is shorter than that, so each pass reads ESPN (and MLB's
  feed) fresh: the scoreboard, each game's plays, baseball's pitches and
  hockey's rink. A pass fetches up to four games' plays at once instead
  of one after another, so a full Sunday slate doesn't slow the pass. A
  fresh file about every 8 to 10 seconds while a game is on. Nothing
  changes when no game is on, and none of it costs odds credits.
- **On the page.** The live game page asks every 5 seconds and the Live
  tab every 5 seconds while a game is in progress. When nothing has
  changed the server answers "not modified" with no download, and the
  page doesn't redraw, so it doesn't jump under your thumb. The live game
  page had also been polling twice as often as it meant to (two timers
  running side by side); it runs one now.
- **What's left.** Our part is now about 8 seconds typical and under 20
  at worst. ESPN's own feed is what we read, so the site can't run ahead
  of ESPN's gamecast; and a streamed broadcast often runs behind live
  itself.
- **The Record, one more fold.** On the live NFL record the verdict still
  repeated two things the same screen already said: the "Combined with
  the Most Likely board" line under the Combined card at the top, and the
  Most Likely sentence ("We said 62%, they hit 62% ...") above the Most
  Likely section that leads with it. Each now prints once; the verdict
  keeps its copy when the other one isn't on the page (a window cut to
  the last N days, or no Most Likely rows).

Nothing to install: the box picks it up by itself (the five-minute update
restarts the site for the new loop). Read-only, any time:

```

grep -c '^SCORES_FAST_S = 5' /srv/qellys/launch.py
python3 -c "import json,time,glob;fs=[f for f in sorted(glob.glob('/srv/qellys/web/data/live_*.json')) if '_box' not in f];g=lambda f:json.load(open(f)).get('generated_at');s={f:{g(f)} for f in fs};[time.sleep(2) or [s[f].add(g(f)) for f in fs] for _ in range(15)];print(' '.join(f.split('live_')[1][:-5]+'='+str(len(v)-1) for f,v in s.items()))"
```

The first two print `1` once this version is live. The third takes 30
seconds and prints how many times each league's live file was rewritten
in that time: while a game is on, about `3` for that league (it was 1 or
2), and the five ESPN leagues move together because one pass writes all
of them. With no game on anywhere it prints `0`s, which is right (the
idle pace is every 3 minutes).

**DX. A chat on every game, plays like Real, and the site free to look
around** (step 181). Your "an app that is better than ESPN ... live chats
on each game for the play-by-plays", "The other app ... is called Reel"
(it's Real, realapp.com), and "make the website free, but the only free
things you get access to is the record, the live page ... we should not
give away any picks or props", with your four answers that day.

- **A chat in every game.** Every live game page (football, college,
  baseball, basketball, hockey) has a Chat button; it opens the game's
  room over the page with the score pinned on top. Anyone can read it.
  Posting, reacting and voting need a free account: a username, an email
  and a password, no card. A message can carry the bet you're on, typed
  by you (player, market, side, line, price, book) and drawn as a ticket.
  The rules show once, and so does a pill when new messages land below
  where you're reading.
- **Every play has its own thread, like Real.** Each play in the
  play-by-play has a speech-bubble button with its comment count; tapping
  it opens that play's thread. Big plays (scores, turnovers, home runs,
  long gains) get four reactions (🔥 😮 😂 😩) with counts, and the room
  has All / Big plays / Game talk filters plus a "Who wins?" vote that
  closes at the final.
- **A play feed across every game.** The Live tab has a Games / Play feed
  switch: the feed is every game's big plays, newest first, each opening
  its game.
- **Keeping it civil.** Three messages a minute for a new account (twelve
  after that) and three seconds between messages; the profanity filter
  the feed uses; blocking hides someone in every room; three reports hide
  a message until you look at it (the same moderation door as the feed);
  you can hide any message. Rooms keep 14 days.
- **Free to look around.** With the paywall on, a visitor is no longer
  sent to the plans. They get the Live tab, every game's play-by-play,
  stats and chat, a game's own page with the facts (lines, weather, the
  stadium, injuries, the team shapes), the Record, Fantasy, My Bets and
  the Feed. The tab bar and menu stay, and every paid tab carries a lock
  and still opens the plans. Where a paid section would be (picks on the
  Live tab and the live game page, the game report, plan, scan, scripts,
  every pick on a game's page) there is one card saying what members get,
  with no number from behind it. Picks were already cut out of the files
  a free reader downloads; that hasn't changed.
- **Fantasy free, your call.** This week's points, each player's chance
  to play, the matchup and role changes, the draft kit, our ranks and
  buy/sell are open. His stat line, touchdown chance, the game script and
  the reasons stay paid (they're the prop board in another form), and so
  do the game scripts on fantasy.json. The public files pick this up at
  the next NFL and fantasy builds; until then the "This week" room shows
  its members card.
- **The first screen, like Real's.** A first-time visitor opens on "Create
  your free account" (username first, checked before the account is
  made) with "Look around first →" under it, which goes to Live and isn't
  asked again. A link to a paid page still opens the plans, and anyone
  who signs up without a plan lands on Live, not the plans. Sign-up and
  the forgot-password page print the 21+ and 1-800-GAMBLER line, since
  those screens hide the footer.

    thread.
