**DZ. Referral codes: $6.25 off for both sides** (step 183). Your "users,
when they have a paid account, they should be given a referral code ...
the new user would get 25% off 1 month, and then the person that referred
them would get 25% off their next month."

- **Why $6.25 and not "25%".** A quarter of the $25 month is $6.25, and
  both sides get it as that amount on every plan. The three plans are one
  Stripe product, so a 25%-off coupon on the yearly plan would take 25%
  off $225 ($56.25) for a referral meant to cost $6.25.
- **The member's side.** Every paying member gets a code (Settings →
  Membership → Invite friends) with a link that carries it
  (`qellysbook.com/?ref=CODE`), Share and Copy buttons, and how it's gone:
  joined, paid, credited. When a friend's membership goes active (their
  first real payment; a trial doesn't count), $6.25 goes on the member's
  Stripe balance, and Stripe takes it off their next invoice. Several
  friends stack, with no limit. If the member isn't paying at that moment,
  the credit waits until they are.
- **The new member's side.** The code rides in on the link, or goes in the
  sign-up's optional "Friend's referral code" box, or into Invite friends
  later before their first plan. Their checkout then carries a $6.25
  coupon (Stripe makes it the first time it's needed), on top of the
  3-day trial when the plan has one. The plans page tells them it's
  coming.
- **What it refuses.** Your own code, a second code on one account, and a
  code after an account has ever subscribed. Stripe idempotency keys mean
  a repeated webhook can't pay the same friend twice. One person with two
  email addresses can't be stopped without keeping card details, which
  the Privacy Policy says we don't do, and it doesn't pay: the second
  account spends $18.75 to earn $6.25.
- **The legal pages caught up.** The Terms gained §5.10 (referral credits:
  no cash value, one per account, can be ended for new referrals). The
  Privacy Policy now names everything an account can hold. It had been
  missing the Feed, friends and messages, phone alerts, the two emails
  and the game chat; now they're all listed, with who sees each and how
  long it's kept. Deleting an account now also removes its chat messages,
  its own alert rows and its referrals. "Download my data" includes its
  chat messages and referral code. A test now fails if a new table
  holding someone's data ships without the policy naming it.

Nothing to install, and no new keys. The Stripe secret key that's already
set makes the coupon and the credits. Read-only, any time:

```
curl -s https://qellysbook.com/js/account.js | grep -c 'async function invFill('
curl -s -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}\n' https://qellysbook.com/api/account/referral
```

The first prints `1` once this version is live; the second prints `401`
(it needs a signed-in account).

Then on your phone, signed in: Settings → Membership → Invite friends shows
your code. Send yourself the link in a private tab: the sign-up's referral
box comes filled in.

```

cd /srv/qellys && nice -n 19 bash tools/catchup.sh EA 2>&1 | tee /tmp/ea.txt

**EB. Live injuries and birthdays on the game, like Real** (step 185).
Your "It has live injuries. It shows players' birthdays and stuff like
that."

- **Live injuries.** Each live game's file now carries ESPN's own injury
  list for both sides, which ESPN keeps current during the game. A status
  that appears after the box first looked at the game (a player who went
  down, or one whose status changed) shows under the score with a red
  INJURY tag and the time, for example "Alvin Kamara (NO) — Questionable,
  Ankle · 6:42 PM". The list as it stood when the box first looked is
  the baseline, so opening a game in the third quarter can't flag the
  whole pregame list as new. It's the status word and body part ESPN
  files, never a written note.
- **Birthdays.** Every league's roster file now keeps each player's birth
  date, from the same feeds as before (Sleeper for the NFL, ESPN for the
  NBA and WNBA, the NHL's own, MLB's own). A game shows "🎂 Aaron Rodgers
  (PIT) turns 43 today" under the score when it's someone's birthday,
  counted on the game's own day in Eastern time.

Nothing to install. Birthdays appear once each league's roster build runs
again (they run on their own schedule), and injuries on the next live
game. Read-only, any time:

```
curl -s https://qellysbook.com/js/detail.js | grep -c 'function pbpNewInjuriesHTML('
python3 -c "import json,glob;print(' '.join(f.split('rosters_')[1][:-5]+'='+str(sum(1 for t in json.load(open(f)).get('teams',{}).values() for q in t.get('players',[]) if q.get('born'))) for f in sorted(glob.glob('/srv/qellys/web/data/rosters_*.json'))))"
python3 -c "import json,glob;fs=glob.glob('/srv/qellys/web/data/pbp/*.json');print(len(fs),sum(1 for f in fs if json.load(open(f)).get('injuries_live')))"
```

The first prints `1` once this version is live. The second prints, for
each league, how many players on its roster file carry a birth date: `0`
until that league's roster build has run again, then most of the roster.
The third prints the live game files and how many carry an injury list;
during games most do, and with no game on both are low or `0`.

The check in one go:

```
cd /srv/qellys && nice -n 19 bash tools/catchup.sh EB 2>&1 | tee /tmp/eb.txt
```

**EC. Sign up with a phone number, like Real** (step 186). Your "you can
either make an account with an email or a phone number. So we should add
that option as well."

- **What it does.** Sign-up gets "Use your phone number instead":
  username, mobile number, then "Text me a code", the code, and Create
  account (with the referral box and the 21+ box like the email form).
  The sign-in card gets "Sign in with your phone number". A phone account
  has no password; it signs in by a texted code each time. Settings shows
  the number instead of an email.
- **It's off until you switch it on.** It needs a Twilio account with a
  Verify service. Twilio texts and checks the code, so the code never
  touches our server. Until the three values below are set, nobody sees
  the phone option.
- **Cost, and the fences on it.** Twilio Verify charges per successful
  verification plus the carrier's text fee, about 5 to 6 cents a sign-in
  in the US (check Twilio's pricing page for today's number). An open
  "text this number" button is how SMS-pumping fraud runs up a bill, so
  it only texts US and Canadian numbers, at most 3 codes to one number
  and 6 from one address an hour, and Twilio's own Fraud Guard sits
  behind that.
- **Privacy.** The policy now says a phone account keeps its number for
  signing in and nothing else, and that Twilio receives the number to
  text the code. Nothing is ever emailed to a phone account.

To switch it on: in Twilio's console make a Verify service (Verify →
Services → Create, any name), then put the three values on the box. Each
is typed at the prompt, never pasted into a file. Then restart:

```
cd /srv/qellys && sudo ./deploy/setenv.sh TWILIO_ACCOUNT_SID
cd /srv/qellys && sudo ./deploy/setenv.sh TWILIO_AUTH_TOKEN
cd /srv/qellys && sudo ./deploy/setenv.sh TWILIO_VERIFY_SID
sudo systemctl restart qellys
```

Read-only, any time:

```
curl -s https://qellysbook.com/api/account/me | grep -o '"phone_signup": [a-z]*'
```

It prints `"phone_signup": false` until the three values are set and the
site restarted, and `"phone_signup": true` after. Then on your phone, in a
private tab: sign up with "Use your phone number instead" and your own
number.

**Which value is which** (2026-10-26: the Account SID went into the Verify
slot too). The Account SID starts with `AC` and sits on the console's home
page with the Auth Token beside it. The Verify SID starts with `VA`: in
the console, Verify → Services → the service you made → **Service SID**.
A value of the wrong shape now keeps the phone option off rather than
showing a button that fails, and this names any value that is missing or
the wrong shape (it never prints a value):

```
cd /srv/qellys && sudo python3 -m engine.smsverify --check
```

To put the right one in (it replaces the old value), then restart:

```
cd /srv/qellys && sudo ./deploy/setenv.sh TWILIO_VERIFY_SID
sudo systemctl restart qellys
```

**It needs a paid Twilio account** (2026-10-27: a free trial shows only
the Account SID, the Auth Token and an API key, no Verify service). A
trial only texts codes to numbers its owner verified, five at most, and
Twilio offers the Verify service once the account is upgraded (a card, a
starting balance and a business profile Twilio approves). Until then the
phone option stays hidden and email sign-up carries on; the check above
says where the `VA…` value comes from. The API key is not needed: the
site signs its requests with the Account SID and the Auth Token.

The check in one go:

```
cd /srv/qellys && nice -n 19 bash tools/catchup.sh EC 2>&1 | tee /tmp/ec.txt
```

**EF. Big moments by push** (step 189). A new switch in Alerts → Yours:
"Big moments in games I follow". With it on, a walk-off, a
late go-ahead score or any play our moment rating puts at 8.5 or more
(about a 35-point swing in the win chance) in a game of a team you follow,
or by a player you follow, taps your phone: "Walk-off: ATL 4–5 NYM ·

game. It's the score, so it's free; at most four from one game; the moments
already on a game when the box first reads it are never sent (a restart
can't replay a game); quiet hours (11pm–9am Eastern) hold it and it expires
after twenty minutes, so the night's moments are dropped, not sent at nine.

**EG. Call it** (step 190). A game inside the game, free: on every live
game page, under the field, a "Call it" card asks how the next one ends.
Baseball: Hit, Out, Strikeout or Walk for the batter up (open until his
second pitch). Football: Touchdown, Field goal, Punt or Turnover for the
drive (open through its first two plays). Basketball (NBA, WNBA): Three,
Two or Free throw for the next points. Hockey: Goal, Save, Blocked or
Missed for the next shot. Those two open after each score or shot and lock
two plays later; a call still open at the final is void. The game's own file says
how it ended a few seconds later; the card shows if you were right, your
count for the game, your streak and best, and the room's top five callers.
A call changes while it's open and never after; an at-bat that ends on an
error or a drive the half ends is void (counts for nobody, breaks no
streak). Signed-out readers see the question and "Make a free account to
play". No money, no prices, nothing from our model. The calls are kept per
account, named in the Privacy Policy, exported and deleted with the account.

Nothing to install. Read-only, any time:

```

curl -s https://qellysbook.com/api/callit/soccer/1
cd /srv/qellys && sudo -u qellys python3 -c "import sqlite3; from engine import accounts as A; c=sqlite3.connect(f'file:{A.DB_PATH}?mode=ro', uri=True); t=c.execute(\"SELECT 1 FROM sqlite_master WHERE name='callit_calls'\").fetchone(); print(c.execute('SELECT COUNT(*), COUNT(won), COALESCE(SUM(won), 0) FROM callit_calls').fetchone() if t else 'no calls yet')"
```

The first prints `1` once this version is live; the second
`{"error": "no such game"}` (soccer isn't a Call it league, so the new
server refuses it by name); the third the calls made, graded and right so
far, or `no calls yet`.

**EJ. The game chat, out of the corner and rebuilt** (step 193). Your "it's
hidden in the corner of the live page ... and the actual chat looks

  three messages, the room's who-wins split, and a gold **Join**.

- **On a computer the chat docks on the right under the site's own bar**,
  so the header and search stay in reach; on a wide screen (1440+) the page
  moves over so nothing is covered.
- **The messages read like a chat app**: one author's run grouped under one
  name, names in their own colour, reactions shown only once somebody used
  one, and the react / Reply / ⋯ tools on hover with a mouse and on a tap on
  a phone instead of under every line. A reply shows what it answers in one
  line; a shared bet is a ticket with a gold edge.
- **The composer is one pill**: + for a bet, the message, a send arrow.

**EM. An account to use the site, and the paid side on its first screen**
(step 196). You asked for an account to be the way in, like Real's first
screen, and then: "show our paid features on the sign up site". Built from
what the store apps do (Real, Sleeper, Duolingo, Spotify, the FT's "view a
sample"):

- **Signed out, every page opens the sign-up first.** The plans, the
  Record, Log in, a password reset and checkout stay open. A link someone
  sends (a game, the feed) waits and opens the moment the account exists,
  and the sign-up says so ("the game you were sent opens"). "Look around
  first" is gone while this is on.
- **The sign-up shows what members get**, under the form: tonight's picks
  as locked cards with only their count per league, last night's record,
  Free beside Member, the newest graded pick in full (won or lost, the
  Record's own row), the record, the three plans (a tap keeps the plan and
  checkout opens right after sign-up) and the free trial's days. Every
  number comes off the public feed below; none is written into the page.
- **Right after a new account picks its teams**, one screen offers the
  membership ("Start 3 days free") with "Continue with the free account" as
  a full button. Once, and never to anyone who chose a plan already.
- **From the free site:** "Get picks" in the top bar, a strip on Live with
  today's member pick count, and a locked tab (Home, Picks, Ask) opens the
  plans saying what that tab holds.

It is on whenever the paywall is. If you want strangers to look around

And back on: `cd /srv/qellys && sudo ./deploy/setenv.sh --unset QB_ACCOUNT_WALL && sudo systemctl restart qellys`.

**EN. The game chat's buttons, and a plans page that shows the free side**
(step 197). Your two reports:

- **"The buttons to get to the game chat glitch out and throw errors."**
  Two causes, both fixed. Step 196's members strip on Live named its loader
  `mbLoad`, which is My Bets' own loader's name; once the chat loaded, it
  replaced My Bets' — so opening the chat threw an error and drew half a
  sheet, and My Bets broke with it. And the chat card under the scoreboard
  was rebuilt every couple of seconds (on every play and every chat poll),
  so a tap could land on a card that had just been replaced. It is now one
  card for the whole visit that only changes its words. A new test fails
  the build if two scripts ever share a name again, and another opens the
  chat from every door in a real browser.
- **"The paywall page makes it seem like everything is still locked."** The
  plans page now opens on **Free to watch. Members get the picks.** with
  two cards side by side: **Free account** (what's free, and a **Create a
  free account** button) and **Member** (what the plan adds, price, trial).
  The top bar says **Sign up free** beside Log in. A plan tapped before an
  account goes to the sign-up with the plan kept, and checkout opens right
  after; it used to send people to the log-in page. Signed in without a
  plan, the free card says "You have this" with the way back to the games.

**EO. The free trial's reminder** (step 198). A member on the 3-day trial
now gets **one email about a day before it turns into the $25 charge**:
when the trial ends (Eastern time), what is charged then, and how to stop
it (account page → Manage billing → Cancel; cancel before then and nothing
is charged). It goes once per trial, never to someone who already
cancelled, and never to a phone-only account, which has no address. With
no mail relay set up it waits instead of being lost, and a send that fails
is tried again 30 minutes later. The Live tab says the same in a line at
the top while the trial runs, in the member's own time, gone once they
cancel. The Terms (§5.3) now promise the email, and the Privacy policy
names the one date it keeps so it goes once.

It runs inside the billing sweep (every 30 minutes, only with Stripe set
up). The box restarts into it on its own once it pulls this push (up to
45 minutes if a board sweep is running); to start it now:

cd /srv/qellys && sudo systemctl restart qellys

**ES. The house posts the big moments in each game's chat** (step 202).
When a play in a live game rates 8 or more (a go-ahead score late, a
walk-off, a big swing in the win chance), the site's own account posts it
in that game's chat — "Go-ahead: PIT 21–17 CIN. Joe Burrow — Passing
Touchdown, Q4, 2:01. Rated 8.7." — on the play's own thread, so the whole
room sees the same moment and replies to it there. It wears the site's
mark and a gold tint. At most six a game; plays already on a game when the
box first looks at it are not posted, so a restart never replays a game.

It posts only as the site's own account: verified, with "qellys" in its

```

(Put the site account's real email where it says site@example.com.) It is
the server's change, so it starts with the same restart as EO. Read-only,
any time — whether the site's own account exists, and each recent game's

   ```

   ```

   ```

2. Phone sign-up (EC): **skip it for now** (Ethan, 2026-10-27: "There is
   no service sid, just a account sid and authentication token and an
   option to create a api key … I don't have a paid twillo account so I
   don't get the VA key thing"). Right: a free Twilio trial has no Verify
   service to copy, and it only texts codes to numbers you have verified
   yourself, five at most, so it could not sign anybody else up anyway.
   The site keeps the phone option hidden until all three values are
   right, and email sign-up works as it is: nothing is broken, and
   nothing needs a restart. The API key is not needed at all.

   Optional tidy-up: the Verify slot still holds the Account SID from
   2026-10-26. It is harmless (the site treats it as not set); this clears
   it:

   ```
   cd /srv/qellys && sudo ./deploy/setenv.sh --unset TWILIO_VERIFY_SID
   ```

   If you ever want phone sign-up: upgrade the Twilio account (a card and
   a starting balance; Twilio also asks for a business profile before it
   texts any number), then Verify → Services → Create new (any name, e.g.
   Qellys Book), copy its Service SID (it starts with `VA`), and:

   ```
   cd /srv/qellys && sudo ./deploy/setenv.sh TWILIO_VERIFY_SID
   sudo systemctl restart qellys
   cd /srv/qellys && sudo python3 -m engine.smsverify --check
   ```

   Every code texted is billed by Twilio, a few cents each.

   `Qellys_Book` were set up on 2026-10-07, so skip the claim. The big

**EZ. The Fantasy room, round one** (step 208). Your ask: "make sure we're
using all our tools and data … and that the fantasy room is actually a
useful tool … I don't know how fantasy works … look at other apps … invent
more things". The audit, what the big apps do and the full plan are in
`docs/FANTASY.md`. Built this round:

- **Fantasy 101**, the first thing on the Fantasy page: the game in plain
  words, how points work, the week's rhythm (waivers, Thursday, Sunday's
  inactives), where to start, and a glossary (PPR, flex, FAAB, handcuff,
  stream, floor and ceiling, Q/D/Out/IR). Close it and it stays a
  one-line bar.
- **Team defenses (D/ST) projected.** Almost every league starts one and
  the site valued every defense at zero, so your lineup could never pick
  one. Now each defense's week comes from the opponent's Vegas total, both
  teams' sack rates and turnover rates. It shows in This week (a new D/ST
  filter), in your lineup and matchup (scored on your league's own defense
  scoring on Sleeper), and in pickups to stream. Free accounts see the
  points; members also see the expected sacks and takeaways.
- **Your week, as a to-do list**, at the top of your league desk: a
  starter who won't play (out or on a bye) and who to start instead, the
  other lineup fixes with the points each gains, a questionable starter
  with his kickoff time, your win chance, the best pickup for your roster,
  next week's byes, and a trade both teams gain from. Tap a line to jump to
  its section.
- **Pickups for your roster**: each free agent ranked by what he adds to
  YOUR best lineup this week (not the league's best player), who he'd
  replace, a FAAB bid range where your league bids from a budget (a rule of
  thumb, and it says so), and the bench player to drop.

On the box, one measurement for the defenses: it replays 2023–2025 week by
week and checks whether the projection orders defenses better than each
defense's season average. Read-only, a minute or two; paste what it
prints:

```

file, `data/models/dst_fit.json`):

**FA. Start A or B?** (step 209). The question every manager asks every
week, "who do I start?", had no tool: each player's week was its own row.
Fantasy → This week now opens on a "Start A or B?" card. Type two to four
names (or a defense), or tap them from your roster once your league is
linked, and pick PPR, half or standard. It answers:

- **The call**: who to start, and how often he outscores each of the
  others (from the same projection and measured spread the league desk
  uses for your win chance).
- **The clock**: a questionable player is decided about 90 minutes before
  his own kickoff, when his team names its inactives, and everyone locks
  at his own kickoff. If the questionable one plays first, it says "Wait
  on him: active, start him; out, start the other." If he plays after the
  other, it says the other locks first, so the call is on his chance to
  play, and to keep a later-game bench player ready.
- **Floor and ceiling** when the points are close: who is the safer start
  (favoured this week? take him) and who has the bigger week in him (the
  underdog? take him). With your league linked it says which you are.

Free accounts get all of that; members also see each player's stat line,
touchdown chance and the reason behind his number. Ask answers "Should I
start X or Y?" with the same call.

```

- **Your playoff chance**: the rest of the regular season played 2,000

  get in; 3–3: 61%".

  team's, the record without schedule luck) and a **lucky/unlucky** tag
  for wins above or below what that says, and each team's playoff and
  first-place chance.
- **Last week in review**: your score against the best lineup you could
  have set (points left on your bench), your costliest call ("starting X
  over Y, −15.2; the other way, you win the game") and your best one, and
  the league's awards: top and lowest score, biggest blowout, closest game,
  unluckiest loss, luckiest win, sharpest lineup, most left on the bench,
  player of the week, best player on a bench.

**FC. Lineup alerts by push** (step 211). The one mistake every fantasy app
guards against: a starter left in your lineup who is out or on a bye. The
to-do list on your desk said it, but only if you opened the desk. Now your
phone says it.

To turn it on (Sleeper leagues): Alerts → Alerts on this phone → under
"Yours", **My fantasy lineup: a starter out or on a bye**. Then open
Fantasy once, so your league desk loads: that is how the site learns which
Sleeper league is yours. Every fifteen minutes the box reads the lineup you
actually have set there and checks each starter against this week's byes
and injury report (and Sleeper's own status). A starter who is out, on IR,
suspended, on a bye, or doubtful (usually sits) gets you one alert, once a
week per news: "Ja'Marr Chase is out. Still in your Sunday Funday lineup.
Tap to see who to start instead." The tap opens Fantasy, where the to-do
list names the replacement. Quiet hours (11pm to 9am Eastern) hold it to
the morning. The alert names the player and his designation (both public)
and never a projection.

What the site keeps: while the switch is on, your Sleeper league's ID and
your Sleeper user ID (to read the lineup you set). Turning it off deletes
them within fifteen minutes; deleting your account deletes them at once.
The Privacy Policy (§2.7) says so, dated 27 October 2026.

**FE. NBA and NHL tonight in fantasy points** (step 213). The last item on
the fantasy list: "NBA and NHL fantasy from the props we already project."
Every night the basketball and hockey builds already project each priced
player's points, rebounds, assists, threes, steals, blocks and turnovers
(hockey: goals, assists, shots and blocked shots). Each build now adds
them up in daily-fantasy points and writes the night beside its board
(`fantasy_nba.json`, `fantasy_wnba.json`, `fantasy_nhl.json`).

- **The Fantasy page** gets a tab, "NBA & NHL tonight", while a slate is
  tonight's (it is not there on a night with none): every player best
  first, his team, the opponent and the tip-off time, a switch between the
  sports, a game picker, and hockey's positions (C, W, D).
- **The scoring** is said on the page: 1 a point, 0.5 a three, 1.25 a
  rebound, 1.5 an assist, 2 a steal or a block, −0.5 a turnover; hockey 8.5
  a goal, 5 an assist, 1.5 a shot on goal, 1.3 a blocked shot. Bonuses
  (double-doubles, hat tricks) are not counted, and goalies are left out:
  their wins and goals against are not projected.
- **Each part** is the board's own projection; a stat the board does not
  price is the player's season average (steadied by last season's early
  on) and drawn dim. A player listed out is left out.
- **Free and paid:** the points are free; the stat line under them is the
  members', like the football week.

before-and-after pictures: the dashboard draws itself more than once while a

Live now card showed. The same page could show it on one visit and not the
next, and a league switched mid-load could keep the last league's card up
until the next refresh. Now the newest draw always wins, and an older one
that comes back late draws nothing (tests/test_the_newest_deck_wins.py).

**FY. Yearly at month two — built, off until you switch it on** (step
233). From the ideas list: a member on the monthly plan who has reached
their third bill sees "Switch to yearly — save $75 a year" once, on
Settings → Plan, with "Not now" to put it away. "See the switch" opens
Stripe's own page confirming the change: the $225 price and what this
month's bill credits, before anything is charged. The date a subscription
began comes from Stripe, read off the updates the site already gets, so it
fills in for current members the next time they open the site or at their
next bill. The privacy policy now names that date.

Two things to switch it on, both yours:

1. In Stripe (dashboard, while signed in): **Settings → Billing → Customer
   portal → Subscriptions**, turn on **Customers can switch plans**, add
   the Qellys product's three prices (monthly, 6 months, yearly), and for
   proration pick **Prorate charges and credits** with **Invoice
   prorations immediately**. Save. Without this Stripe refuses the switch
   page and the member sees an error instead.

2. On the box, the switch and a restart:

```
cd /srv/qellys && sudo ./deploy/setenv.sh QB_YEARLY_OFFER 1 && sudo systemctl restart qellys
```

To turn it off again: `sudo ./deploy/setenv.sh --unset QB_YEARLY_OFFER`, then
`sudo systemctl restart qellys`.

**FZ. Gift a month — built, off until you name a gift price** (step 234).
From the ideas list: Settings → Gift a month, $25 once on Stripe's
Checkout (not a subscription), and the buyer gets a code like
GIFT-7KQ2-M9XR to send; the friend enters it under Redeem a code and has a
month of everything from that day. A code works once. Codes are made only
on Stripe's word that the gift was paid (the webhook you already have, or
the buyer's return from Checkout, checked with Stripe), and a gift never
turns into a plan for the buyer. The privacy policy names the new table.

One thing to switch it on, yours: in Stripe (dashboard, signed in),
**Product catalogue → Add product**, name it "A month of Qellys Book (gift)",
price **$25.00, One-off** (not recurring), save, and copy the price's id
(it starts `price_`). Then on the box, paste it when asked:

```

**GA. Passkeys — built, nothing to switch on** (step 235). From the ideas
list: sign in with Face ID, Touch ID, a fingerprint or Windows Hello, no
password and no texted code. Signed in, **Settings → Passkeys → Add a
passkey** makes one on that device (the phone asks for your face or
finger once); after that the sign-in card's **Sign in with a passkey**
signs you in with it. The box keeps only the half of the key that checks
a signature; your face, your fingerprint and the private half never
leave the phone. Every check the WebAuthn standard lists happens on the
box (our challenge, once and within three minutes; our site; you present
and verified; the signature; a use count that moved on), and the
privacy policy names the three new tables. Deleting an account deletes
its passkeys.

Nothing to set: it works on https://qellysbook.com as it stands (the www
address already forwards there). Not inside the iPhone app yet, where the
row and the button stay hidden: Apple only lets an app's web view use a
site's passkeys once the app carries a passkey entitlement for the site
and the site names the app back, a change in Xcode and here; say if you
want it.

To try it, on your phone in Safari or Chrome: sign in, Settings →
Passkeys → Add a passkey, then Sign out and tap **Sign in with a
passkey** on the sign-in card.

Read-only checks, any time. The first prints `1` once this version is
live; the second prints `qellysbook.com`, the name passkeys are made for
(it asks for a sign-in challenge, which lapses unused in three minutes):

curl -s https://qellysbook.com/js/account.js | grep -c 'function pkHTML('
curl -s -X POST -H 'Origin: https://qellysbook.com' -H 'Content-Type: application/json' -d '{}' https://qellysbook.com/api/passkey/login-options | python3 -c "import json,sys; print(json.load(sys.stdin).get('rpId'))"
```

16. **The first visit to a sport** (2026-10-09, "now make the first visit
    to a sport faster too … everything needs to be faster"). Nothing to
    run for it to work. One read-only check, from the box, tells me how
    long the server itself takes to answer and how many bytes a phone
    really downloads for each board; paste what it prints:
    ```
    for u in /api/recommendations /api/mlb/recommendations /api/cfb/recommendations /api/nhl/recommendations /data/recommendations_picks.json /data/record.json /data/rosters_cfb.json; do curl -s -o /dev/null -H "Accept-Encoding: gzip" -w "$u %{http_code} wait %{time_starttransfer}s total %{time_total}s %{size_download} bytes\n" "https://qellysbook.com$u"; done
    ```
    "wait" over half a second on a board means the box is the slow part,
    not the phone. The record and roster files shrink by about 40% after
    their next rebuild (they are written compact now).
    (Done 2026-10-09: every "wait" was 0.16–0.22 s, and the split showed
    why — DNS 3 ms, connect 4 ms, the secure handshake 158 ms, then the
    first byte 4 ms after it. The server answers in 4 ms. The handshake
    number turned out to be curl's own, not the server's — see the end of
    step 17.)

17. **The slim record and the slow handshake** (2026-10-09). Nothing to
    run for the slim record to work: after the box pulls, the next settle
    pass writes `record_head.json` beside `record.json` — the same record
    without the sections only the Record page draws (about half the
    bytes) — and Home reads that instead. Two read-only checks; paste
    what they print:
    ```
    for f in record record_head; do curl -s -o /dev/null -H "Accept-Encoding: gzip" -w "$f %{http_code} %{size_download} bytes\n" "https://qellysbook.com/data/$f.json"; done
    uptime; free -m; swapon --show; vmstat 1 5; cat /proc/pressure/cpu /proc/pressure/memory; ps -eo pid,ni,rss,pcpu,comm --sort=-rss | head -8; for i in 1 2 3 4 5; do curl -s -o /dev/null -w "tls %{time_appconnect}s first byte %{time_starttransfer}s\n" https://qellysbook.com/data/record_head.json; done
    ```
    The first line should show `record_head` well under `record`; a 404
    on it only means no settle pass has run since the pull. In the second
    check, `vmstat`'s `si`/`so` columns above zero mean the box is
    swapping (the handshake waits on memory read back from disk); `st`
    above 5 means DigitalOcean is lending our one CPU to a neighbour,
    which no code change fixes; the `some avg10` lines above 10 mean the
    box is short of memory or CPU. Five handshakes all near 0.15 s is a
    steady cost; one slow and four fast was a cold start.
    (Done 2026-10-09. The slim record is 31.8 KB on the wire against
    136.7 KB for the full one. No swapping (`si`/`so` 0), no steal (`st`
    0–1); the CPU is full of the builds and chores, all at nice 10, so
    Caddy and the server still go first. The handshake is NOT the server:
    curl itself spends 0.08 s of CPU per run on this box, and from here
    Cloudflare's own site and Google's take as long as ours —
    0.119 s, 0.128 s, ours 0.102 s. A browser keeps its certificates
    loaded, so a visitor pays one round trip to New York instead. Also
    learned: Cloudflare is NOT in front of the site any more — no `cf-ray`
    or `server: cloudflare` header — whatever "Behind Cloudflare" in
    DEPLOY.md says. Don't chase the handshake again from the box; time it
    from a computer that is not the box if it ever matters.)

18. **College Most Likely** (2026-10-09, your words: "we need to fix that,
    and we need to make changes now"). The box's loss audit said the
    staked college picks were honest (claimed 63%, hit 63%) but bought at
    prices needing 65%, and the whole college board was too sure on
    unders, rushing yards and its bolder-than-the-books picks. Three
    things, in order:
    ```
    cd /srv/qellys
    sudo -u qellys python3 -m engine.posspread --sport cfb
    sudo -u qellys python3 -m engine.scouthist --sport cfb --dry-run 2>&1 | tail -40
    sudo -u qellys python3 -m engine.likelyctx fit --sport cfb --dry-run 2>&1 | tail -30
    ```
    The first SAVES college's measured spreads — WR catches ×1.20, WR
    yards ×1.35, RB rushing yards ×1.50, each better in 4-5 of 5 held-out
    seasons (the weekly job would save the same on its own). The other two
    are read-only: the scout's history and record checks, which matched
    nothing on the first run because of a join bug fixed in `e50ad436`.
    Nothing to run for the price rule: from the next college build a pick
    whose chance is below its price's break-even is graded on paper, not
    staked, and its card says "Price too high to stake".

19. **College teammate-out and new QB** (2026-10-10, "do the teammate-out
    and new QB stuff for college now"). Built and wired; nothing moves
    until college's own games pass the NFL's rule. Measure, read, then

    ```

    sudo -u qellys python3 -m engine.cfb.lineup --dry-run 2>&1 | tail -60
    sudo -u qellys python3 -m engine.cfb.lineup

    ```
    The first prints every case (a teammate ranked above or below him

    2026-10-10 (first game back, rushing overs on a team expected to
    trail, and the rest) — a pick carrying one is lowered by what history
    proved, never below its price.

25. **Five new NFL markets** (2026-10-10, "Yes add all of them"): a QB's
    pass + rush yards, a back's rush + rec yards, a kicker's points and
    field goals, a defender's tackles + assists. Each was measured before
    it went on (marketfit.py, held-out 2025: 0.683, 0.629, 0.673, 0.700,
    0.621). They are on the NFL odds request (22 credits an event, was 17)
    behind the drop-and-retry guard. After the next NFL build, see which
    the books priced and which reached Most Likely:
    ```
    cd /srv/qellys && sudo -u qellys python3 -c "
    import json, collections, os
    p = 'data/built/recommendations.json' if os.path.exists('data/built/recommendations.json') else 'web/data/recommendations.json'
    d = json.load(open(p)); new = ('pass_rush_yds','rush_rec_yds','kick_pts','fg_made','tackles_ast')
    recs = [r for r in d.get('recommendations', []) if r.get('market') in new]
    print('built:', collections.Counter(r['market'] for r in recs))
    print('book-priced:', collections.Counter(r['market'] for r in recs if r.get('has_market')))
    print('most likely:', collections.Counter(r['market'] for r in d.get('most_likely', []) if r.get('market') in new))"
    ```
    A market at zero under "book-priced" with the others filled means the

26. **Make "Forgot password?" email people** (2026-10-11, Ethan: "we
    have to have a forgot a password feture"). The Forgot page is built
    and the Gmail settings are on the box, but the test said **"Could not
    reach smtp.gmail.com on port 587"**: DigitalOcean blocks the mail
    ports (25, 465, 587) on newer accounts and often will not lift it. So
    the site now also sends through **Resend**, an email service the site
    talks to over normal HTTPS (port 443), which DigitalOcean does not
    block. Free for up to 100 emails a day. About 10 minutes:

    **A. The box has the new code.** The top line should read `Mail: send
    through Resend` or something newer; if older, wait five minutes:
    ```
    git -C /srv/qellys log --oneline -1
    ```

    **B. Make the Resend account and connect the domain:**
    1. Go to **https://resend.com**, sign up with qellysbook@gmail.com.
    2. **Domains → Add Domain**, type `qellysbook.com`, then click
       **Sign in to Cloudflare** and allow it. Resend adds its DNS records
       to Cloudflare for you. Wait until the domain says **Verified**
       (usually a few minutes; refresh the page).
       No Cloudflare button? Choose manual setup: Resend lists 3–4
       records. For each one, in Cloudflare → qellysbook.com → **DNS** →
       **Add record**, copy the Type, Name and Value exactly, and leave
       Proxy status as **DNS only** (grey cloud).
    3. **API Keys → Create API Key**, name `qellys site`, permission
       **Sending access**, then **Add**. Copy the key (it starts with
       `re_`); Resend shows it once. Don't send it to me.

    **C. Put it on the box** (`ssh` in), one line at a time:
    ```
    cd /srv/qellys
    sudo ./deploy/setenv.sh QB_RESEND_KEY
    sudo ./deploy/setenv.sh QB_MAIL_FROM noreply@qellysbook.com
    sudo ./deploy/setenv.sh QB_MAIL_REPLY_TO qellysbook@gmail.com
    sudo systemctl restart qellys
    ```
    The first one asks for the value and shows nothing while you type:
    paste the `re_...` key and press Enter. Emails come from "Qellys Book
    <noreply@qellysbook.com>", and anyone who hits Reply reaches
    qellysbook@gmail.com. (The Gmail settings you already set can stay;
    while QB_RESEND_KEY is set they are not used.)

    **D. Test it** (your own inbox):
    ```
    cd /srv/qellys && sudo python3 -m engine.mailer test you@example.com
    ```
    It should say `Sent to ...`. If it says the domain is not verified,
    wait for B2 to show Verified and run it again. Then on the site: Log in
    → **Forgot password?** → your account's email → **Email me a link**.
    To see the site knows mail is on (`"reset_by_email": true`):
    ```
    curl -s http://127.0.0.1:8000/api/account/me; echo
    ```
    Optional: an email when the site's service crashes:
    `sudo ./deploy/setenv.sh QB_ALERT_EMAIL qellysbook@gmail.com` then
    restart.

    **Rather send from the Gmail itself?** Ask DigitalOcean (Help →
    Support): "Please unblock outbound SMTP (port 587) on my droplet. It
    sends password-reset emails for my own site through my Gmail
    account." If they do, switch back with
    `sudo ./deploy/setenv.sh --unset QB_RESEND_KEY`,
    `sudo ./deploy/setenv.sh QB_MAIL_FROM qellysbook@gmail.com`, restart,
    and run the test in D.

    Notes: at most 90 reset emails go out a day (under Resend's free 100)
    and 3 an hour to one address. The morning/nightly digests stay off on
    Resend's free plan and on Gmail; a mailing list would use up the
    day's password resets. Until C is done, the Forgot page tells people
    to email qellysbook@gmail.com, and you can make a link by hand:
    ```
    cd /srv/qellys && sudo -u qellys python3 -m engine.accounts reset-link someone@example.com
    ```
    Email that link only to the account's own address.

27. **Hometown games for NFL picks** (2026-10-11, Ethan: "one signal that
    I want to add for our NFL bets is hometown returns"). A player's ROAD
    game within 50 miles of where he was born now raises a scout flag on
    his card ("an under in a road game in his hometown (Detroit, MI) …").
    His own team's home games never count, nor neutral sites. Like every
    flag it moves no number until the five-season replay proves it, by the
    same fixed bar as the rest (3+ points worse on that side, in both
    halves, 100+ games each). Both sides are flagged, so the replay finds
    whichever is real: if hometown players rise to it, unders on them get
    lowered; if the occasion gets to them, overs do.
    Birthplaces come from ESPN's rosters and Sleeper's player file; the
    map is the US Census place list (downloaded once).

    **A. Build the birthplace table** (a minute; prints who it placed and
    a sample of skill players born near another team's stadium — glance
    at the sample to check it looks right):
    ```
    cd /srv/qellys && sudo -u qellys python3 -m engine.hometown
    ```
    If it says the Census map could not be loaded, paste me that line.

    **B. See what five seasons say** (read-only; a few minutes):
    ```
    cd /srv/qellys && sudo -u qellys nice -n 19 python3 -m engine.scouthist --sport nfl --dry-run
    ```
    Paste back the `hometown:` line and any `PROVEN  hometown_…` line. A
    proven line starts lowering those picks after the weekly run saves it;
    none means the flag stays a note on the card. The weekly run rebuilds
    the birthplace table by itself from now on.

    **Run 1 result (2026-10-11):** 2,281 of today's players placed;
    Sleeper's file carries no birthplaces; history held only 142 hometown
    games — too few for the bar. Ethan: "ok push further." So A now also
    looks up every player in our 2021+ logs whom no roster lists today,
    by his ESPN id (from nflverse's season rosters), once each. **Run A
    again** — the first time takes 10–20 minutes (it walks ESPN's player
    pages slowly), later runs seconds. It prints `past players: …`. Then
    run B again: it now also prints **every hometown market's raw numbers**
    (hit rate against the usual rate, and every over / every under
    together) — shown, not applied, so you can see which way it leans
    even when it is too thin to prove.

    **Run 2 result (2026-10-11):** 2,792 players placed (555 past players
    from their ESPN pages) and 195 hometown games scored. Every over
    together hit 1.3 points above the usual rate and every under 2.3
    points below it, which is a lean toward players rising to the
    occasion. Catches overs ran +5.2% on 172 games. Nothing cleared the
    bar, which needs 100+ games in *each* half on one market, so nothing
    moves. The weekly run re-tests it as 2026 adds games. Nothing more to
    run.

28. **The extras** (2026-10-11, "add the three optional extras"):
    * **Crash alerts** are on: when the site's service fails, an email

      you are done. If not, **Add record**: Type `TXT`, Name `_dmarc`,

      caps password resets at 90.

      (below). The card shows how far he has to go and links the report.

    ```
    Paste back the `spots, every market pooled` block and any `PROVEN`

    **C. Add an incentive when a report gives one.** Example: a report

    cd /srv/qellys && sudo -u qellys python3 -m engine.incentives list

30. **Why the NFL board is red, and whether bigger units would help**
    (2026-10-11, Ethan: "we have won so many most likely bets … I feel
    like we should be up more money"; "save the code for when I'm
    home"). Read-only, seconds:
    ```
    cd /srv/qellys && sudo -u qellys python3 lossaudit.py --sport nfl
    ```
    Paste back, for **The one board** and **Most Likely, staked**:
    * `-- by price` — which price range loses.
    * `-- by our chance against the price` — the picks whose shown chance
      covered what the price needed to break even, against the ones it
      didn't. If the covered row clearly beats the other, the college
      rule (stake only what covers its price) is worth turning on for
      the NFL. That's your call; nothing changes until you say so.
    * `-- bigger units on the same … picks` — the same picks at 1u flat
      against 3u Top pick / 2u Strong / 1u Worth a look. Sizing multiplies
      a result. It cannot turn a loss into a win.
    **Run 2026-10-15** (read in step 109): the one board covered its price
    on 367 picks for −10.0% and missed it on 552 for −6.0%; staked Most
    Likely −1.2% against −3.2%. The rule is not turned on. Bigger units:
    −7.6u flat against −14.2u tiered per 100 picks.
