75. **Steals, blocks and turnovers on the NBA board** (2026-10-14, you:
    "Do 1-7 and the ones we download but don't use"). AG runs it. Both NBA
    ingests have stored the three since 2026-10-13; nothing priced them.
    THE ODDS: the NBA request asks for `player_steals`, `player_blocks`
    and `player_turnovers` on every event, main lines only, behind the
    drop-and-retry guard every new key gets: 19 credits an event, was 16.
    The WNBA's request is unchanged (its season is over, and the call was
    the NBA's). A MISSING ROW IS NOT A ZERO: a game ingested before
    2026-10-13 has no row for them, and reading it as a night with none
    would price every under at a certainty. So each reads only the run of
    his newest games whose box stored it, and a player with fewer than
    three such games gets no prop (the build prints how many), one with
    fewer than ten is pulled toward the book's price as hard as any thin
    sample; AG's first command fills last season. PRICING: as counts, the way rebounds are
    (exact, never tighter than Poisson); turnovers' spread starts from a
    stated prior (0.60) and the build's own fit measures all three from
    the league's logs, as every stat's. They clear the high-hold bar
    threes already clear (4 points over break-even, not 2): low counts
    where one more decides the bet, hung at a heavier hold. THE MATCHUP:
    the defence read was never measured to reach one player in these, so
    the model has no matchup step for them and the board says so (it no
    longer prints `matchup moved our number +0%`, here or for the combos).
    The card under the pick still says what tonight's opponent gives up —
    steals given up, shots blocked, turnovers forced, ranked — and that
    the model leaves it out; AG's second command measures it, on the same
    bar as the other four (400 games, two standard errors). A journaled
    pick settles off its own box-score row; the live tracker, the parlay
    checker (tier 3, and kept out of compounding tickets, as threes are),
    the slip grader and the slip reader, the player page's stat chips,
    Ask and the replay all know the three, and Most Likely shelves them as
    `Steals, blocks & turnovers` once each is measured to rank. PRA AND
    THE COMBOS ON MOST LIKELY, found on the way: the ranking walk reads
    stored rows and no ingest stores a sum, so PRA (on the walk's list
    from the start) and the combos bought on 10-13 were never measured and
    could never reach Most Likely. The walk now adds each game's parts up
    (only games where every part is on file), for ranking only: the
    calibration, form and player-memory fits still read stored rows, so no
    price moves. Your call: those three fits list PRA too and have never
    found a row for it either; switching them on would move PRA's prices,
    so they are left as they were. **Answered 2026-10-14: yes** ("Ya do
    the self tuneung"): step 77, AI. Fixed on the way: the replay divided by
    zero on a game with no stored count before it; it skips it now, the
    three-game floor every prop has. Budgets: the first visit is 610.97
    of 611 KB (no bump). Pins changed, told to you, nothing loosened:
    `tests/test_nba_prices_the_two_stat_combos.py` (the NBA's credits 16
    to 19, and its slate list ends with the three),
    `tests/test_alt_lines.py` (19 an NBA event), `tests/test_hoops_ladder.py`
    and `tests/test_wnba.py` (the NBA's markets beyond the WNBA's now
    include the three), `tests/test_mlb_likely.py` (a fourth basketball
    shelf, `counts`), `tests/test_basketball_props_are_replayed.py` (its
    stand-in logs gain the three, from their own random stream so the
    other markets' numbers are unchanged, and "every market is priced and
    graded" still holds for all eleven). New:
    `tests/test_nba_prices_steals_blocks_turnovers.py`.
76. **Round prices on every UFC fight the model prices** (2026-10-14, the
    same call: "UFC finish times … would be our own fair prices to shop
    by hand"). AH checks it. Whether a fight goes the distance was already
    priced, off the method model; rounds were refused, because pricing
    them needs when a finish lands and the dossiers hold no finish times
    (the script's written hazard is never to price a round). Since
    2026-10-14 `ufc_fights` holds how and when each fighter's recent bouts
    ended, so `engine/ufc/rounds` prices them: when a finish lands, given
    a finish, is the spread of finish times over every stored bout
    scheduled for the same rounds (each bout once), leaned toward the two
    fighters' own inside-the-distance fights, each worth one fight against
    ten fights' worth of the league; times the method model's chance of a
    finish, so the rounds always agree with the distance price. Over and
    under 1.5 and 2.5 rounds (3.5 and 4.5 too in a five-rounder) and the
    round it ends in. Fewer than 120 stored finishes at a fight's length
    and it carries none, never the written prior; three rounds is assumed
    where the feed names none, as the card's duration read says. Not
    measured against results, and said on the read: fair numbers to shop,
    tier 3, never staked, never journaled, never a fight's best market;
    a number under 1% or over 99% is left off. They ride on the fight's
    own `Every market this fight implies` list, which a passed fight's
    card now draws too (only picks drew it). No pins changed. New:
    `tests/test_ufc_round_prices_from_finish_times.py`.
77. **The NBA's self-tuning learns on the board's own model** (2026-10-14,
    you: "Ya do the self tuneung", to step 75's call). AI runs it now;
    Wednesday's refit does it by itself. WHAT WAS WRONG, found on the way:
    the deep fits (temperatures, player memory, recency dial) and the Most
    Likely ranking walk all replay a sport's past games through one door
    (`logwalk.walk`), and that door handed the NBA and the WNBA to the
    FOOTBALL chain, a form blend with no minutes in it. The NBA board then
    applied what those fits learned (its temperatures, its player
    corrections) to its own minutes × rate projection: corrections
    measured on one model, applied to another. The door hands basketball
    to the board's own model now (`engine/hoopsreplay.walk`): each past
    game priced off the twenty before it exactly as the live build hands
    them to the board (his minutes, his starts, the nights he sat, a count
    read only where its box stored it), with the league's fitted spreads,
    at the naive line, -110 both ways, and no gate in the way, so a fit
    learns from every game the model projected. It walks a market's most
    recent 30,000 games, to stay inside the box's fifteen minutes a fit.
    PRA, the three combos and steals, blocks and turnovers are fitted with
    the rest now; the WNBA board prices five and fits five, the same way.
    THE RECENCY DIAL declines basketball: the board's projection has no
    blend of windows to turn, so formfit.py prints `not fitted` for both
    leagues and retires any NBA or WNBA dial it stored off the football
    chain (none reached the board, but the Record page listed them as its
    learning), and the weekly log says `not fitted` rather than `nothing
    adopted`. WHAT MOVES: at the next refit every NBA temperature and
    player correction is refit on the board's model, each under its own
    bar (200 games and a held-out test for a temperature; a causal Brier
    gain on 200 games for the memory), so NBA prices move then, PRA's
    among them, and the Record page's NBA learning rows describe the
    board. The ranking numbers that open Most Likely shelves are measured
    on the board's model against the same 0.60 floor, so a shelf can open
    or close on the first run. A CRASH, FOUND BY THIS WALK AND FIXED: a
    player with none of a count in every game his window holds (a guard's
    blocks over the few games whose box carried them, a centre's threes)
    projects zero, and pricing zero divided by zero; nothing above it
    caught that, so the first such prop a book offered would have stopped
    the whole NBA slate. It prices zero now, and the board refuses the
    prop with the reason it gives any number too far from the book's. The
    Lab's replay of a count now keeps all twenty games' minutes, as the
    build does (it kept only the games that carried the count). No page
    code changed. Pins changed, told to you, nothing loosened:
    `tests/test_nba_prices_steals_blocks_turnovers.py` (its ranking test
    pinned the old `sums=True` door and "the price fitters read stored
    rows only"; every basketball walk reads the summed entries now, with
    his minutes beside them and a game missing a part kept as empty
    rather than dropped). New: `tests/test_nba_self_tunes_on_its_own_model.py`.
78. **No apology on the pick page** (2026-10-14, you, with a screenshot:
    "Every time I click on a player page, for the most likely bets, it
    shows this"). AJ checks it. The line in brackets said what it was:
    `ResizeObserver loop completed with undelivered notifications.` That
    is the browser's notice, not an error in the page: when the sizes the
    page watches (its charts, its tables, the header) settle across two
    frames instead of one, the browser says so and delivers the rest a
    frame later. Nothing failed to draw. Safari raises it while a pick
    page settles; the page's own error catcher, which puts up the apology
    whenever a script throws, read it as a throw. It lets that notice go
    now (both wordings, Safari's and Chrome's older one), and every real
    throw still gets the line — checked in Chromium both ways. The
    page's own size watchers were checked too: none changes a size while
    the browser is delivering, so there was nothing of ours to fix behind
    it, and Chromium did not raise it on these pages in my checks. The served copy
    grows by 28 bytes: the first visit is 610.995 of 611 KB, under the
    budget, no bump. No pins changed. New:
    `tests/test_a_resize_notice_is_not_a_broken_page.py`.

81. **The site came up empty** (2026-10-14, you: "we are no longer
    showing players. There's no most likely bets. There's no edge bets.
    There's no players showing up in the search page ... make sure all
    our new pushes are being pushed correctly"). AM checks it. WHAT
    HAPPENED, as far as the code shows it from here (the box itself is
    AM's to tell): the page takes a board's picks off once the board is an
    hour old, and search and every paid board are the app answering, so
    the box had stopped finishing its board builds and stopped answering.
    Two things from the 13th did that. THE MEMORY: the baseball and
    basketball chart backfills (steps 65 and 66) read every game of a
    night's budget into memory before storing one (400 baseball
    play-by-plays at about 2 MB each once parsed; 300 basketball games
    with their box scores), most of a gigabyte each, inside the 1,600 MB
    the whole service shares with the web server and the builds. The
    chart filler starts them every two hours while a chart table is
    empty, and the daily chores every night, so the box kept walking into
    its memory wall, where the kernel either swaps everything and the
    site crawls (2026-08-17, three hours) or kills (2026-10-07, ten
    kills). THE RESTARTS: every push restarted the box at once, killing
    the board sweep and every build in it; thirty pushes between 17:00 on
    the 13th and 11:30 on the 14th (UTC), most under an hour apart, so
    the boards late in the sweep's order never finished. THE FIXES. (1)
    Both backfills read, fold and store eight games at a time
    (`CHUNK`), so a run holds about 16 MB, not the night; a chunk where
    nothing could be read ends the run and leaves the rest for the next,
    so a host refusing us is not asked again forty times. Same nightly
    budgets. (2) Every child the app starts (the board builds, the chores
    lanes, the chart filler, the fitters) now marks itself first for the
    kernel's OOM killer (`engine/childproc`: oom_score_adj 500, raised by
    the child itself, no privilege needed) and is niced as before, so if
    memory runs out again a child goes, never the web server that answers
    search and the boards. (3) A push no longer restarts the box
    mid-sweep. The app says whether a board sweep is running
    (`data/cache/sweep.json`); while one is, the updater pulls (the new
    page is served at once) but holds the restart and leaves
    `data/cache/restart_wanted.json`, which stops the app starting
    another sweep, so the restart lands when that sweep ends: never more
    than 45 minutes after the pull, never held for a sweep whose process
    is gone or that has run past 50 minutes, and at once for an app too
    old to say (so this push's own restart is immediate, as before). The
    app ignores a request older than 50 minutes, so a broken updater
    cannot freeze the boards. Also: the Line Shopping view read
    `state.data.odds_status` before checking there was a board, and threw
    when opened before the board loaded; it reads the board once,
    guarded. Checked: the 22 opt-in browser tests (the site in Chromium,
    every view tapped before the board answers at phone width, every
    chart) and the full suite. First visit 610.983 of 611 KB, app 494.475
    of 495. Pins changed, told to you, nothing loosened:
    `tests/test_a_stuck_chore_never_stops_the_boards.py` and
    `tests/test_show_boards.py` pinned the old one-line niceness
    (`os.nice(10)`) at two of the three places children start; they pin
    the helper now, and the new test pins the niceness and the OOM score
    in a real child. `tests/test_cold_open.py` pinned Line Shopping's old
    partial guard (`(state.data || {}).market_scan`); it pins the
    read-once guard and now also fails on any bare `state.data.` in that
    function. New: `tests/test_chart_backfills_hold_a_chunk_not_the_night.py`,
    `tests/test_children_go_before_the_server.py`,
    `tests/test_the_deploy_waits_for_the_board_sweep.py`.

83. **The hit tracker is there before you scroll to it** (2026-10-14,
    you, with a pick page reading "The hit tracker didn't load. Try
    again in a moment.": "Also we are running into issues where the hit
    tracker won't load or takes forever too load. It should be snappy
    and basically be loaded already when you scroll down too go view
    it"). AO checks it. WHAT WAS SLOW, measured on a full-size season
    here: the hit tracker itself builds in about 40 ms, and every
    request for it then stopped to ask MLB's servers for the league's
    rosters to find his photo — 357 of a 413 ms request where they
    cannot be reached, and on the box, whenever the six-hour roster
    file had aged out, one request (or thirty-one while MLB ignores the
    one-call version), each allowed thirty seconds, inside the reader's
    request and once for every chart the page asked for at once. A name
    the book prints without its accent read every plate appearance on
    file (350 ms, every time). The page asked for nothing until you had
    scrolled within a screen of the chart, then waited for his role
    chart and tonight's matchup (a cold matchup is a league table, 1.4
    seconds) before drawing the one that leads, and loaded the
    baseball drawing only after the data. And one dropped request — a
    deploy restarting the box, a phone's connection — left "didn't
    load" for the rest of the visit, with no way to ask again. THE
    SERVER: a built chart is kept fifteen minutes as the bytes the
    endpoint sends (`engine/charts.answer_json`, per database file,
    32 MB at most, the oldest read out first); however many readers
    ask for one chart at once, one request builds it and the rest wait
    for that build; "still being filled" and "nothing on file" are
    never kept. His photo is read off the rosters the box last saved,
    whatever their age, and never asked for mid-request
    (`mlbstats.cached_active_rosters`, used by `rosters.face_map`); the
    board build still refreshes those files every sweep. A baseball
    chart carries his photo off his own MLB id. The folded names are
    read once in ten minutes off their own index (25 ms) instead of
    every plate appearance on every request; the matchup's league
    tables are built once however many ask. THE PAGE
    (`web/js/carrychart.js`): the first chart on a page — a pick page's
    — is asked for the moment the page is drawn, its drawing fetched
    beside its data; one further down a list (a search's players) as it
    comes within two screens. His own chart is drawn the moment it
    arrives, with places kept on the switch for Role and Matchup, which
    join it as they land without the chart being drawn again. A request
    that drops, a 5xx or one with no answer in 25 seconds is asked again
    0.8, 2.5 and 5 seconds later (Caddy already holds a request for six
    seconds while the app restarts); a refusal is not. Two asks for one
    chart at once are one request. One that still fails says "didn't
    load" with a Try again button; a chart whose drawing did not arrive
    says so rather than drawing as another chart. While it loads, its
    shape shimmers where it will sit, so nothing under it jumps.
    MEASURED on the real site against a full season here, the chart
    3,000 px down the page, nothing scrolled, a phone-speed connection
    (1.6 Mbps, 150 ms) and the server just restarted: before, nothing
    was asked for until the reader scrolled down to it, and it then took
    3.4 seconds to appear; now it is asked for as the page opens and is
    already drawn when the reader gets there (10 ms after scrolling to
    it). On the server: the hit tracker builds in 40 to 120 ms and is
    sent from its kept copy in about 2 ms; Role 150 ms; Matchup 1.4
    seconds once after each restart (its league table), a millisecond
    after that. Checked: the new test's browser walk (the chart drawn before his
    role arrives with nothing scrolled, Role and Matchup joining the
    switch without a redraw, a dropped matchup asked again, a slot
    further down left alone until it nears the screen, and Try again
    after nothing came back), the hit page's browser walk, the chart
    tests and the full suite. App 494.475 of 495 KB and first visit
    610.983 of 611, unchanged (app.js untouched; the chart module loads
    on first use). Pins changed, told to you, nothing loosened:
    `tests/test_the_pick_page_draws_his_chart.py` pinned the charts
    asked for "as it nears the screen" (`rootMargin: "800px 0px"`, and
    no other way in); it pins the first chart on a page asked for at
    once and a slot further down at two screens (`1600px`), and the new
    shape of the ask that sends his charts together. `tests/test_statlogs.py`
    swapped in a stand-in for the face lookup that took two arguments;
    the stand-ins take the new `offline` one, and the first now also
    fails unless the page's lookup asks for the cached rosters only. New:
    `tests/test_the_hit_tracker_is_there_when_you_get_to_it.py`.
84. **The hit page draws the seven things it left out** (2026-10-14, you,

       game he only pinch-ran in included.
    2. THE RECORD ON THE DAY, under each club's score in the regular
       season, off MLB's schedule. A new table, `mlb_games`: every final's
       park, score, both clubs' records as the schedule gives them, a
       doubleheader's game number and October's series, filled from the
       schedule the nightly backfill already reads (no request of its
       own); a season read before the table was kept, once (2025: one
       request). A record shows only where our own count of the season's
       finals agrees with the league's figure, the record after the game
       or the day before — which also proves no game of that club's is
       missing up to then — and is shown as the record through the game.
       In October the card names the series and the game of it instead.
    3. THE PARK AND VIEW GAME: "@ Yankee Stadium" under the date, and a
       View game ↗ button to the game on MLB.com (mlb.com/gameday/<id>).
    4. BARRELS by MLB's published rule (98 mph at 26 to 30 degrees, the
       window widening with every mph to 8 to 50 degrees at 116), on MLB's
       own exit velocity and launch angle: a Barrel tag on an at-bat, a

86. **Basketball and hockey name a Pick of the Day** (2026-10-14, the
    same ask: step 85 made every league's pick reach the record, and the
    NBA and NHL had none to record). AQ checks it. WHY THEY HAD NONE: the
    day's pick is game markets only (your call, 2026-09-15), and their
    Most Likely boards carry no game market — none is measured for either
    league, and by your rule of 2026-09-02 a market with no figure stays
    off that board; the NBA build prices no game lines at all. That rule
    is about ranking, and the pick does not rank on our model: a row
    priced against Pinnacle answers to Pinnacle's fair, which needs no
    figure of ours. So `potd.game_lines` hands the selector the game lines
    the odds pull already hangs on each game (no new credits): every side
    of every moneyline, spread and total Pinnacle quoted two ways at the
    line a soft book posts, at the best soft price and the book posting
    it. NOTHING IS WAIVED: today's game, not under way, a real book, the
    band, the 50% floor, 2% EV and the ceiling on a gap too big to trust.
    The rows are never put on the Most Likely board; the build hands them
    to the relock beside it, so a pick named from one stays the day's pick
    when a later build likes another game. The card prints no "our
    number" on them (none of our models priced them), and the record keeps
    the fair the card printed as its claim. The NBA build drops preseason
    games before any of this, so its first pick can come on the 20th.
    Football and baseball do not get the lines: their Most Likely boards
    carry measured game rows, and a row that board turned down on the
    merits must not reach the pick by another door. UFC still names no
    Pick of the Day — the fight card is its own page with its own book —
    and the Record page no longer gives it an empty section: the record
    now carries a book for every league that names a pick, empty or not
    (`ledger.POTD_LEAGUES`), and the page draws only those. (Since step 97
    the fight card names one too.) No pin
    loosened; the empty-section test changed again to match (an entry
    draws "Nothing settled yet", none draws nothing). New:

88. **The Status page stops calling half-hour-old boards stale** (2026-10-14,
    you, with a screenshot at 12:42: "Also everything is stale" — the NFL
    3 minutes old, then MLB 25, NBA and WNBA 22, NHL 20, college 36, all
    red). AS checks it. THOSE AGES WERE NORMAL: the box rebuilds every board
    in one sweep, NFL first, and a sweep takes about half an hour, so a board
    is up to that old before its turn comes (the screenshot's times are one
    sweep from 16:06 to 16:30 UTC and the next reaching the NFL at 16:39).
    The page calls a board late past twice the box's median cycle plus a
    minute, and that median was wrong in two ways. A SKIPPED CYCLE WAS
    TIMED: after every restart the startup build holds the build lock for
    about thirty minutes, and the refresher, waking every minute, skipped
    and still timed itself at a fraction of a second; thirty of those in a
    median of twenty put the line at its 8-minute floor, and it took hours
    of real sweeps to recover. And THE TIMING DIED WITH THE PROCESS: every
    deploy started from nothing, and until two sweeps were timed the page
    used a 16-minute floor. Four pushes that afternoon kept it there. The
    same median told the page, and the box's daily check, how long a
    heartbeat may be silent before the loop counts as dead, so the
    page could also say "The refresh loop is not running" while it ran.
    Now only a cycle that swept (or failed trying) is timed, the startup
    build is timed as the sweep it is, and the heartbeat carries the
    timings (`cycles_s`) so the next process starts from them. AND A DOCS
    PUSH NO LONGER RESTARTS THE SITE: every pull restarted it, the
    runbook's included, and a restart costs the boards a sweep. A pull
    that changes only `docs/`, `tests/`, `audit/` or a Markdown file at the
    top is pulled with no restart (nothing there is loaded when the site
    starts; what `docs/` holds that it reads at all, it reads when asked);
    code waiting from an earlier pull still restarts, and `--boards` says
    a docs-only gap is not a failed restart. ONCE, NOT AGAIN: the first
    hour after this lands, the page can still read red while the box times
    its first two sweeps. No model or board changed, and no page file
    (app 494.479 of 495 KB, first visit 610.987 of 611). New:
    `tests/test_a_deploy_does_not_make_every_board_read_stale.py` (16
    checks: the refresher run for real with a held lock, a pending restart
    and the updater's request, the startup sweep, the seed from a
    heartbeat and the ones it refuses, the heartbeat written and read back,
    the updater on real git repositories for docs, tests, top-level
    Markdown, code, Markdown inside `deploy/`, and code still waiting under
    a docs pull, and the two copies of the rule held equal). No pin
    changed.

    it the way baseball always has: the scan's lean decides where it has
    one; where it has none, the projection's own Matchup step — the one

    3% or more the other way, and says nothing in between. A market the
    defence never moves says so: a quarterback's carries show the run
    defence and keep it out of the number, so their step is flat ("not
    enough to call") and they still top out at Strong. Touchdowns read their touchdown matchup as before. The
    parlay checker's read asks the same question the same way. The Most
    Likely page's explanation now says the football version in football
    (it had only baseball's words for this rule): app.js 494.489 of 495

    * **who wins**: the board's own chance, the model held toward the
      market as every UFC price is;
    * **how**: KO/TKO, submission, decision, or inside the distance, in
      the model's own split of each fighter's wins;
    * **the distance and the rounds**: goes the distance, over or under a
      number of rounds, and the round it ends in, in the shape the round
      prices already use (step 76).
    How and when are fair numbers the board shows to shop and never
    stakes, and the leg says so. **Two legs on one fight are priced
    together**, off the same outcomes, never as two separate games: "A
    wins" with "A by decision" is just the decision, "A wins" with "B
    wins" (or "goes the distance" with "A by KO") cannot both happen and
    the card says so, and the 2,000-night replay draws one ending a night.
    A bout the board passed on before pricing (no dossier, no fight data,
    no two-sided price) gets no number, with that reason; a round leg
    needs the round prices. **A saved fight slip settles off the live
    fight feed**, on the Live tab and by the box's slip watch: a winner
    settles a fighter's moneyline, and the live feed now also records how
    a bout ended where ESPN's scoreboard says so (KO/TKO, submission or
    decision; AX checks it) for the method, distance and round legs. A bout the feed shows with no winner (a draw, a no
    contest) settles nothing; your book settles those. The reader in Ask
    now knows UFC slips (the sport and seven fight markets). New:
    `engine/slipfight.py`;
    `tests/test_ufc_fights_in_the_parlay_checker.py` (14 checks). No test

100. **Fresh slip prices never touch the board's budget** (2026-10-14, you:

    less. The 20-credit daily cap stays on top. A game past either keeps

    may never see if its game starts before the next build. TWO CAUSES,
    both fixed: (1) the audit trail's label (`ledger.audit_reason`, which
    names why each graded row changed) was written into a shared table at

    settle pass held the journal's write lock from its first line to its
    last; the label is now each connection's own (a temporary table and
    trigger), which locks nothing, and still lands on exactly that
    connection's rows; (2) the settle loop committed once, after the last
    bet; it now commits each bet before reading the next. No pin changed.

110. **A settled moneyline or total keeps its close** (2026-10-15, found
    in your `closecheck.py --sport nfl` paste: 31 of 31 NFL moneylines had

    the bet and looked up no close, because the stores the props close
    from (the purchased history, our own prop snapshots) carry no game
    lines. The board's own do: `engine/lineopen` writes each game's numbers
    every build and stops at kickoff, so the row left standing is the last
    line the board read before the game, at the best price across the
    books (the price a moneyline is taken at). The settle now banks it
    (`ledger._game_close`): a moneyline on its side's price, so its price
    CLV reads; a total on the number; a spread nothing, as before. The game
    is found the way its grade finds it, the clubs under either spelling on
    the slate's day; a football week also takes the day either side (a
    Monday night game the history files on Tuesday). A doubleheader banks
    none: the store keeps one row per pair per day, so which leg's price
    it ended on is a guess. A price the board marks as dated (over six
    hours old; it shows them for up to two days) no longer moves that row,
    so it can't pass for the close. WHICH BOARDS WRITE THESE LINES
    (corrected 2026-10-15 with step 111): a game row is kept only when it
    carries both moneylines. The NFL, MLB and the NHL boards' rows do; the
    NBA's and the WNBA's do from step 111 on (their games carried no
    moneyline until then, so they kept no line); college's board games still
    carry none, so college keeps no line here and its game-line bets settle
    with no close, as before. Only the NFL board marks a price as dated: the
    others' close is the last line they showed, whatever its age. The close
    repair (`launch.py --repair-closes`) doesn't read these
    lines, so it now keeps a game line's close instead of clearing it, and
    prints those apart as KEPT. NOT BACKFILLED: the 31 stay without a
    close. The lines stored before today can't show their price was
    current, and a close of unknown age is worse than none. Every game-line
    bet that settles from now on gets one, the NFL's from Thursday night's
    game on. If the store can't be read, the settle
    still grades every bet and its log says "game-line closes skipped".
    New: `tests/test_a_game_line_keeps_its_close.py` (10 checks). One
    other test file changed, the way it asks of every new function that

111. **The NBA live page, in your render** (2026-10-15, with your two
    renders: "the live play by play screen for nba looks like shit ...
    figure out what live data we can get too make it look like an actual
    play by play screen like ball tracking or possessions"; BH). A
    basketball game, the NBA's or the WNBA's, now opens its own page in the
    render's layout. THE SCOREBOARD: both clubs with their records, the
    score, the quarter and the clock; the bonus, team fouls and timeouts
    only when ESPN's scorer's-table block carries them; a ball beside the
    side that has it. THE ARENA (your art, the crown at centre court) with
    four views. Live: the last shot flies from where it went up to the
    rim, once, when it is new to the page; the seven before it stay on the
    floor, fading; an arrow at centre court points the way the ball is
    going; the last play sits in a caption; a run of six or more unanswered
    gets a badge. Shot Chart: every shot of the game on the floor, made or
    missed, either side or both, with each side's makes over its tries.
    Player Tracker: the five on the floor with their points, rebounds,
    assists and fouls. Game Flow: the margin over the game, lead changes,
    ties, each side's largest lead. Then three panels: every play by
    quarter, newest first; Team Stats (bars in each club's colour), Player
    Stats, a flat shot chart with each zone's makes, and the quarter-by-
    quarter line with the runs; Game Odds (the board's spread, moneyline and
    total, and our win chance), our picks on the game, and Trends (the pace
    against the board's total, shooting, the run, any side without a field
    goal in three minutes). On a phone the scoreboard is one line a side,
    crest and score either side of the clock, and the arena shows the half
    the last shot went up in, so the court is big enough to read.
    WHAT THE LIVE DATA CAN AND CAN'T DO. Nobody sells us ball tracking: the
    league's own feed, which has it play by play, answers the box 403 (step
    103), and there is no shot clock in anything we can read. ESPN's game
    file has every play in order, who was in it, and where every shot went
    up, so `engine/sources/espnplays` now keeps each play's spot on the
    floor and every name on it, and the game file adds the quarter scores,
    the building and ESPN's scorer's-table block where one exists; the box
    score marks its starters. Everything else the page works out from the
    plays, in the open, and draws nothing it can't stand behind: whose ball
    it is only where a play settles it (a make, a rebound, a turnover, a
    foul, the last free throw of a trip; a missed shot leaves no arrow until
    the rebound); who is on the floor only when every substitution of the
    game is possible under one reading of ESPN's two names (one impossible
    step and the tracker shows the leading scorers instead); an assist,
    block, steal or drawn foul only when the box puts that man on that
    role's side. ESPN's written sentences are not stored and not drawn. The
    win chance is our model's. THE COURT ON THE ART: the four corners of the
    floor were read off the render at full size and a perspective map puts
    every spot in feet on the picture; the render's lines are drawn about a
    tenth larger than a real court, so distances are stretched to match (a
    three lands outside the painted arc). The away side shoots at the left
    basket, the home side at the right. Every mark on the floor sits on a
    dark edge of itself, so a club drawn in gold (the Lakers, the Warriors)
    still reads on the wood. The page redraws every twelve
    seconds without the picture blinking or the plays jumping back to the
    top. AND THE NBA'S GAMES NOW CARRY THEIR MONEYLINES on the board

112. **The NHL live page, in your render** (2026-10-15, with your renders:
    "figure out what data we can use for the live play by play screen for
    nhl like live posetions and puck tracking"; BI). A hockey game now opens
    its own page in the render's layout. THE SCOREBOARD: both clubs with
    their records, the score, the period and the clock, the power play with
    its time left (`PP BOS 1:40`), shots on goal. THE ARENA (your rink, the
    crown at centre ice) with five views in a bar under it, as the render
    has them. Live: the newest event ringed where it happened; the plays
    since the last faceoff joined in the order they happened (never across
    a whistle), the earlier ones fading; a shot flies to the net, once, when
    it is new to the page; a goal lights the lamp; an arrow at centre ice
    when a play has settled whose puck it is; the last play in a caption
    with the player's face. Shot Chart: every attempt, a goal ringed, a shot
    on goal filled, a miss hollow, a block crossed, either side or both,
    with each side's goals over its shots and our expected goals. Ice
    Tracker: the hits, takeaways, giveaways, faceoffs and penalties where
    they happened, a chip for each kind with its count. Player Tracker: each
    side's skaters by ice time with goals, assists, shots and plus-minus,
    and each goalie's saves. Game Flow: each side's running count of shot
    attempts, each goal a dot on the scorer's line. Down the right: every
    play by period, newest first; Game Stats (goals and shots by period,
    faceoffs, power plays); Trends (the last five minutes' attempts, the
    power play, a side without a shot on goal in five minutes, a pulled
    goalie, our expected goals, the goalies); our picks on the game. Under
    it: Team Stats (bars in each club's colour), Player Stats, a flat shot
    chart and the flow; the lineup (forwards, defence, the goalie), every
    skater's ice time, the goalies with our goals saved above expected; Game
    Odds (the board's puck line, moneyline and total, and our win chance)
    and Live Props. On a phone the scoreboard is one line a side and the
    rink shows the half the newest event is in.
    WHAT THE LIVE DATA CAN AND CAN'T DO. The league's own play-by-play (the
    keyless feed the expected-goals model already reads) places every event
    on the ice in feet, with who was in it and how many skaters each side
    had; ESPN's hockey plays carry none of that. `engine/sources/nhlrink`
    turns it into the game file's `rink` block, with the league's box score
    (ice time) and our expected goals on each unblocked attempt, and the
    live scores loop writes it on every pass for up to eight games
    (`livescore_build.nhl_rink_pass`; the file's own clock is left alone, so
    the play feed's rotation is unchanged). Nobody publishes where the puck
    is between events, live: the Live view draws the events where they
    happened, in order, which is as close to puck tracking as any public
    feed gets. Whose puck it is is said only after a faceoff won, a takeaway
    or a giveaway; a shot, a hit or a block leaves it loose and the page
    says nothing. The power play is the feed's own when it carries one,
    else worked out from the penalties on the sheet (a minor ends at the
    first goal against it), and `PP` with no time when it can't be told.
    Who is on the ice is not in the play-by-play; the shift charts might
    say, if they are kept up during a game, which BI checks, and until then
    the page says so and lists every skater's game. The league does publish
    tracked puck and skater positions for the seconds before each goal (its
    goal visualizer); BI checks that feed's shape before anything is built
    on it. A play's club is its player's, off the roster (a blocked shot
    counts as the shooter's attempt and the blocker's block), and only the
    league's short codes are kept, never a sentence. Overtime is five
    minutes in the regular season and twenty in the playoffs. THE RINK ON
    THE ART: the render is a panorama (short end zones, bowed near boards),
    so twenty-five points were read off it at full size (the faceoff dots,
    both nets, centre ice, the blue lines and the red line at five depths,
    the boards) and a smooth map through them puts every spot on the
    picture; every row is turned so the home side shoots at the right-hand
    net. THE CLUBS' COLOURS follow your render: the Rangers in their blue,
    lifted until it reads on the dark panels; a navy far too dark gives way
    to the club's next colour (Washington's red, Edmonton's orange), black
    gives way (Pittsburgh's gold, the Kings' silver), and the away side
    takes another of its colours when the two would look alike; every mark
    on the ice sits on a dark edge. The basketball page keeps its own rule.
    If the page fails to load or draw, or a game has no `rink` block yet,
    hockey stays on the old rooms. The new files (`js/rinkcast.js`,
    `css/rinkcast.css`, the art at two sizes) load only when a hockey game

    unchanged; like the basketball page's, they are served as written, not

115. **Trust the price more where our chance runs far above it? The check,
    its bar written first** (2026-10-15, the last idea waiting in H, from
    step 109's pastes: on the one board the picks whose chance ran furthest
    above the price were the furthest wrong, claimed 65%, hit 53%, 367 NFL
    picks; BK). `pricetrust.py`, read-only: it opens the ledger read-only
    and streams our odds snapshots, and writes nothing. THE QUESTION: would
    giving back part of the gap between our chance and the market's
    de-vigged chance, where ours ran above it, have been closer to what
    happened? THE RULE, one family: shown = ours − w × max(0, ours − market
    − T), w from 0 to 1 in tenths, T 0, 5, 10 or 15 points; w 0 is "change
    nothing". THE MARKET'S CHANCE is the one at the moment the pick was
    posted: the field's de-vigged fair journaled with the pick where there
    is one (the Edge book's rows), otherwise rebuilt from our own snapshots
    (each book's last quote at the pick's own line in the six hours before
    it was posted, never after it and never in play, both sides quoted, the
    median of at least three books, by the same function that journals the
    field's fair), otherwise none, and the pick is counted and left out.
    Never the close, never the price we took with a vig guessed off it. HOW:
    each book and league on its own, week by week; every week with 60
    settled picks before it is scored with the w and T those earlier weeks
    chose. THE BAR, written in the script before any run, as H put it:
    PASSES when every held-out week's log loss is no worse with the rule and
    the pooled log loss is better; NOT YET under three held-out weeks or 100
    held-out picks; otherwise FAILS. Tried on made-up seasons the size of the
    NFL board (about 180 picks a week, five held-out weeks): where the market
    is right and our extra chance is pure overclaim, it passes about three
    runs in four (30 of 40; the test's 20, 16); where our chance is right,
    one in twenty to forty (1 of 40; the test's 20, 1). Where the
    truth is half way between the two, it mostly fails: "every week no
    worse" is strict, so a FAIL means not shown, not disproved. A PASS
    changes nothing by itself; it comes to you with the w and T it chose.
    New: `tests/test_pricetrust_judges_on_weeks_it_never_saw.py` (16 checks:
    a planted overclaim found on weeks that chose nothing, those pass rates,
    honest chances choosing no change, each week chosen only on the weeks
    before it, the rule never raising a chance, quotes after the pick or in
    play or over six hours old never used, an under read as the other side,
    three books or none, an accented name, a thin book refusing to judge,
    nothing written, the bar in the script's own words). No pin changed.
    RUN 2026-10-15 (your BK paste). THE NFL ONE BOARD: NOT YET. 267 of its
    919 settled picks had the market's chance at pick time (all rebuilt
    from the snapshots). Two held-out weeks, W04 and W05 (188 picks), both
    closer with w 1.0, T 0 (log loss 0.698 → 0.686); week 6 is the third.
    Described only, not judged: its 24 picks where ours ran 15 or more
    points above the market hit 37.5%, where we said 75% and the market
    54%. ONE PASS: Most Likely, paper · MLB, three held-out weeks (w 0.0,
    0.1, 0.3; log loss 0.6949 → 0.6941). Brought to you as promised, with
    my advice against it: the gain is about a tenth of a percent, the first
    of its three weeks passed by changing nothing, it is the paper book,
    and the MLB season ends within days. ONE FAIL: Edge picks · MLB, 3 of 7
    held-out weeks worse. The Edge book is already pulled toward the
    market, and more pull hurt. Everything else NOT YET; college had the
    market's chance on 40 of its 667 board picks. Nothing changed.
    COVERAGE, the same evening (BK's second command): which NFL board
    picks the check can see. Receptions 79 of 175, pass TDs 35 of 47, rush
    attempts 28 of 62, completions 24 of 46, pass attempts 17 of 46,
    tackles 9 of 17, field goals 4 of 7; receiving yards 37 of 180,
    rushing yards 19 of 101, passing yards 2 of 61; anytime TD 13 of 134;
    moneylines 0 of 31, kicking points 0 of 10, rush + rec yards 0 of 2.
    Why, by the rules the check was written with: a yardage pick sits on
    the number the shop chose, and three books rarely quote exactly that
    number; an anytime TD's "no" is rarely quoted, and one side alone
    needs a guessed vig; a moneyline is not in the prop snapshots (and the
    board's moneyline chance is already the market's). NOT WIDENED: the
    method was set before the run, and the rule, if it ever went in, could
    act only where the build has the same three-book number, so the check
    measures exactly the picks the rule could move. Tuesday's verdict
    speaks for those picks, not for the yardage, touchdown or moneyline
    picks.

119. **The Toolbox, the college upset meter and My Bets' report card**
    (2026-10-15, the first slice of your "do every one for every sport").
    NONE OF IT CHANGES A PICK. A new page, Toolbox (sidebar under My Book,
    the phone's More sheet, the Features page), free like the Streak: an
    odds converter (American, decimal or a percent), no-vig and hold for up
    to four sides (both the even split and the power method), a parlay of
    up to six legs (what it pays, what the prices say it hits, and its value
    at your own chances; it says same-game legs are not independent), Kelly
    at a fraction, and break-even and value. Above them, each sport's own
    tools; college's is the UPSET METER: every underdog on the board, ranked
    by our chance he wins outright, beside the market's chance with the hold
    taken out. It is the board's own published moneyline number (college
    game cards are shown and never staked), it leaves out a sharp book's
    card and a game under way, and it says "context, not a pick". MY BETS
    now leads with a REPORT CARD once a bet is graded: your hit rate against
    what your prices needed (the sum of each price's own break-even, so
    beating it means beating the book, not a coin), whether that is still
    inside ordinary luck, by sport, kind (props, game lines, parlays) and
    price band, and your closing-line value — from the closes you log, and,
    if you press "Look up our closes", ours: the page sends the sport, date
    and description of your settled bets without a close (never the stake
    or the book) to a new endpoint, `/api/mybets/closes`
    (`engine/reportcard.py`), which answers only from SETTLED picks of ours
    with a closing price, and only when exactly one pick fits (player or

126. **A parlay builder in every board sport's Toolbox** (2026-10-15, one more
    of the sixteen: NFL, college, MLB, NBA, WNBA, NHL). Context; nothing on
    the board moves for it. The reader picks legs off tonight's Most Likely
    board (filtered by game) and sees the chance they all hit WITH the
    correlation between them beside the chance as if they were independent,
    the fair price, the legs' own prices multiplied, each correlated pair
    with the engine's reason, and up to five of the board's legs that move
    with the ones chosen ("stacks with it", an average correlation of 0.15
    or more). THE RULES ARE THE MODEL'S OWN: every pair goes through
    `engine/parlays.relate` (the clash taxonomy and its measured priors) and
    the ticket through `check_ticket`, so a pair the taxonomy kills (the
    same man twice, the pass game with the run game, two receivers splitting
    one catch pie) is refused with its reason, and the ticket stops at the
    sport's ceiling (three legs; two in hockey). THE PRICE: legs with no
    correlation between them (two games) multiply; a linked pair is the
    engine's Gaussian copula (`joint_two`), a linked three with every pair
    positive is the model's own `joint_three`, anything else a fixed-seed
    simulation. Priced whole, a cross-game leg's zero would shrink
    `joint_three`'s one-factor loadings to nothing and lose the correlated
    pair, so the groups are priced apart. A ticket with two legs from one
    game claims no edge: a book prices a same-game parlay with its own
    correlation, never as the legs multiplied, so the page says to compare
    its price with the fair one. Across games it shows what the ticket is
    worth a dollar at the legs' own prices by our chances.
    `engine/parlaybuild.py`, served at `/api/parlay/build` — unauthenticated
    like the ticket check, since it reads nothing but the legs and chances
    the page already holds (the picks themselves stay behind the membership:
    a signed-out reader's builder says so). New:
    `tests/test_the_parlay_builder.py` (7 checks). Two pins changed, each to
    the new line with the builder appended and still an exact match:
    `tests/test_basketball_schedule_spots_and_what_if_he_sits.py` now reads
    `nba: [spotsHTML, whatifHTML, builderHTML]` (and the same for WNBA), and
    `tests/test_hockey_goalies_lines_and_the_xg_race.py` reads
    `nhl: [goaliesHTML, linesHTML, builderHTML]`. app.js did not change (no
    budget moved).
127. **A free weekly pick'em on Social** (2026-10-15, the last of the sixteen:
    NFL, college, MLB, NBA, WNBA, NHL). A Pick'em tab on Social: every game on
    a sport's board, pick the team that wins. A week runs Tuesday to Monday,
    Eastern, so an NFL week (Thursday night to Monday night) is one contest;
    the daily sports add each day's games as the board posts them. Each game
    shows the room's split; the standings rank the week and the season (every
    week of the last 200 days) by wins, then fewest losses, ties sharing a
    rank, the viewer's own row lit. Free: a signed-in account with a Social
    handle, no membership, no entry fee, no prizes. THE RECORD CANNOT BE
    FAKED, the feed's rule: the page sends which game and which team, never a
    result; the game is looked up on the sport's own board, the team must be
    one of its two, and the pick locks at kickoff (or the board's live flag) —
    changeable until then, never after. GRADING is the server's alone, from
    the finals table every bet grades from (`history.db` `games`, opened
    read-only, matched on both teams and the date within a day and oriented
    to the pick's own home and away): a tie is a push, two different finals
    are refused rather than guessed, a game with no final ten days on is void.
    It grades lazily, on the first read of the page after a game is due, like
    the feed's parlays — no scheduled job. `engine/pickem.py`, table
    `pickem_picks` beside the accounts; served at `/api/feed/pickem` (GET,
    open to read) and POST `/api/feed/pickem` (signed in). A deleted account
    takes its picks with it and the account export includes them. Nothing
    reads or moves a model number. New: `tests/test_the_weekly_pickem.py`
    (7 checks). No pin changed; app.js did not change (no budget moved).
128. **The pick'em's NFL week, from your paste on the 16th** (2026-10-16).
    BV read `0 games` with Sunday's and Monday's games on the board. Two
    faults in reading the board's games, both fixed in `engine/pickem.py`:
    the NFL board carries its kickoff as a bare Eastern clock
    ("13:00", nflverse's game time) beside the game's date, and the pick'em
    read only a full timestamp, so it left every football game off; it now
    joins the two with `engine/fatigue.kickoff_instant`, the helper the
    ledger's kickoff map already uses (a week label like "2026-W06" is not a
    day, so that game stays off rather than be guessed). And every game
    carries the scoreboard's status, `scheduled` before the start, which the
    pick'em read as begun, so every game would have been locked; now only a
    game the scoreboard calls live or final has begun, or one past its
    kickoff. The slip watcher also makes the replay's table on its first
    pass rather than at the first open saved slip (BT read "no replay table
    yet" only because no member had a slip open). BR's check read the public
    UFC file, whose fight reads are behind the paywall, so it always printed
    `0 fights`; it reads `data/built/ufc.json` now. B's journal line skips
    the site's own `/api/` requests, which the new parlay builder's `build`
    URLs were flooding. One more check in `tests/test_the_weekly_pickem.py`
    (now 8) and one more assertion in
    `tests/test_the_sweat_meter_and_the_bet_replay.py`. No pin changed or
    loosened; app.js did not change (no budget moved). NOT CHANGED, waiting
    on BW and your yes: the goalies' expected goals (BW part 2).
    BW'S PASTE, the same evening. (1) The pick'em reads `Oct 13 – 19 14
    games, 1 begun` (Thursday's), so the fix is live. (2) The goalies:
    CONFIRMED. Every season the expected goals on every unblocked attempt
    match the goals (2023: 8,070 vs 8,105; 2024: 7,755 vs 7,867; 2025: 8,154
    vs 8,025; 2026 so far 631 vs 638), and the expected goals on shots on
    net alone are about 70% of them (5,731; 5,312; 5,511; 426), so every
    goalie's expected goals against read ~30% short; all 122,520 stored
    misses name the goalie. Where it shows: the goalie ratio is ~1.4 for an
    average goalie instead of 1.0, so nearly every confirmed opposing starter
    hits the 20% cap on skater goals, anytime goal, points and assists, and
    Edge Hunter's goalie lean lifts goals on almost every game; game lines
    barely move (a starter against his own team's goalies, both short alike).
    The record has barely seen it: 4 settled NHL goal/point/assist picks
    (assists unders, 0 of 4). The fix (the misses back in, in
    `engine/nhl/xg.py` and the goalie save map) is written and waits on
    your yes. (3) The NBA log's last lines are the WNBA's run, "no games
    today": no NBA prices until opening night on the 20th, as expected.
    (4) Judge's 66 games are all he played: every one of 2026's 2,461 finals
    is stored (76 to 419 a month, March to October), the backfill read its
    last game on the 14th, and the page caps nothing, so the page is right.
    (5) UFC: 49 fights, 63 of 98 corners carry a path.
129. **The inventions, part 1** (2026-10-16, Ethan: "add everything now.
    1-13 and dont stop till everything is done", after the brainstorm of
    ideas no betting app he could name was using). Each is a reading on the
    pick or game page, labelled with what its measurement says, and moves
    no number. They travel in one private file per board,
    `data/built/ideas_<sport>.json` (`engine/ideas.py`), written by the NFL
    and college builds and served one pick or one game at a time at
    `/api/ideas`, behind the membership like every book's line; the page
    draws it from a new lazy `js/lab.js` + `css/lab.css`, loaded by
    `js/extras.js`. A section that fails is named in the file's `errors`
    and the others ship. (1) **Do the books add up?** (`engine/bookmenu.py`)
    At one book at a time: the passer it prices highest against his own
    receivers' yards (three or more priced), his completions against their
    catches, a back's rush + rec against its two halves, a passer's pass +
    rush against its halves, and the team's touchdown prices (both sides of
    each, de-vigged) against the touchdowns its points imply. What normal
    looks like is measured on the box's stored closes (BX 1); a menu is
    flagged only two spreads from that, and the side it leans to is a
    reading until a held-out test written before the first run says
    otherwise (both test seasons above zero, 150 flagged menus, t ≥ 2,
    +3 points over the fair price). (2) **The market's whole curve**
    (`engine/altcurve.py`): every rung of the alternate ladder priced both
    ways, de-vigged, the median across books, made to fall as the number
    rises, beside our chance at the same rungs (the Most Likely board's own
    rung pricing). "Ends disagree" when the middle agrees and an end parts
    by 6 points. The ladders were never stored, so the builds now keep a
    curve per player and market a day (`alt_curves` in history.db) and BX 3
    scores them as games settle. (3) **How each book sets his line**
    (`engine/bookrecipe.py`): a least-squares recipe per book from his last
    game, last three, season and career to the line it hung, beside the
    recipe that predicted what players did; the pick page shows each book's
    recipe for him beside its line and how much each leans on his last
    game. Measured on held-out seasons (BX 2). New tests:
    `tests/test_the_books_checked_against_themselves.py` (7),
    `tests/test_the_markets_whole_curve.py` (6),
    `tests/test_how_each_book_sets_a_line.py` (5). No pin changed; app.js
    grew by two hooks (a pick-page slot and a game-page slot), inside its
    budget.
130. **The inventions, part 2** (2026-10-16, the same request). (4)
    **Which books copy which** (`engine/bookclusters.py`): for each pair of
    books, on every stored close both priced, how often they hung the
    identical line and how far apart their over prices were when they did;
    average-linkage groups on that (a pair needs 200 shared closes, and two
    books must agree three times in four to be one feed); the prop tape
    (every book's number when it changes, since September) names each
    group's leader and how fast its copies follow. The pick page says how
    many books price him and how many opinions that is, the fair over chance
    counted by book and by opinion, and any group whose leader has moved
    while its copies still show the old number. Nothing on the board stops
    counting books until a held-out test says counting opinions is better
    (log loss, 2,000 props, t ≥ 2). (5) **The injury report, read closely**
    (`engine/practice.py`): every nflverse report the box can read, against
    whether the man played (a snap share above zero) and his snaps when he
    did, by designation and how he practised; and each club's questionable
    rate, pulled toward the league by 20 listings so a small sample does not
    make its own number. The pick page shows a listed man's designation, how
    he practised, how often men listed like him played, and his club's
    record; the game page lists every listed man in the game. The board's
    injury hold is unchanged. (6) **Public attention** (`engine/attention.py`):
    daily human views of each player's Wikipedia page from Wikimedia's own
    free API (a User-Agent, no key, nothing scraped), his article found once
    with Wikipedia's own search; the builds keep the board's players current
    (each at most once a day, a few seconds a build) and `--backfill` fills
    past seasons. The pick page shows his last three days against his usual
    and the month; whether a spike makes his over the expensive side is
    measured on every past close (BY 3). The ideas file now goes beside the
    build's own board (`gate`'s private copy), and the journals run only for
    the real board: a test that runs a build writes its file in its scratch
    tree and keeps nothing in the history database. The menu, recipe and
    clusters measurements load one market at a time as tuples, sized for a
    gigabyte box. New tests: `tests/test_which_books_copy_which.py` (6),
    `tests/test_practice_reports_and_club_honesty.py` (5),
    `tests/test_public_attention_from_wikipedia.py` (6), and one more check
    in `tests/test_the_books_checked_against_themselves.py`. No pin changed.
131. **The inventions, part 3** (2026-10-16, the same request). (7) **The
    air a kick flies through** (`engine/kickphysics.py`): the air's density
    from the ideal gas law — the standard atmosphere's pressure at the
    stadium's altitude (or a measured pressure), the game-time temperature,
    and water vapour when the humidity is known (moist air is lighter); sea
    level at 59°F is 1.225, Denver on a mild day about a fifth thinner, and
    under a roof the building's 70°F. A wind is split along and across the
    field once a stadium's bearing is recorded, and a bearing is recorded
    only with a source link (`--bearing TEAM DEGREES --source URL`; the
    table starts empty). The make chance is a logistic fit on every stored
    field goal (distance, distance squared, wind × distance outdoors) with
    and without distance × the air's density against sea level; the density
    term earns a reading only if it improves held-out log loss over the two
    newest seasons, t ≥ 2, 2,000 kicks, AND its sign is the physics' (BZ 1).
    The game page and a kicker's pick page show the air, the wind and the
    stadium's height; "a 50-yard try plays like" appears only from a fit
    that held. (8) **Rookies and transfers** (`engine/rookies.py`): a
    rookie receiver, tight end or back's last college season (the college
    board's own logs, one school, six games) and his draft slot (nflverse
    rosters; undrafted reads as pick 260), translated by one least-squares
    fit per market into his first NFL month (weeks 1-4, two games). It must
    beat draft slot alone AND his position's rookie mean on the two newest
    classes, paired, t ≥ 2, 60 rookies (BZ 2). For college, a transfer's
    first four games at the new school against his last season at the old
    one, as one ratio per market. The pick page shows his college line and
    the translation, labelled; nothing on the board reads either. New
    tests: `tests/test_the_air_a_kick_flies_through.py` (5),
    `tests/test_rookies_from_their_college_line.py` (5). No pin changed.
132. **The inventions, part 4** (2026-10-16, the same request). (9)
    **Players inside the simulated game** (`engine/playsim.py`). The drive
    simulator (`engine/drivesim`) replays games as two scores with no
    player in them, so every same-game ticket is joined with one number
    per kind of pair (a passer and any receiver; two receivers), whoever
    the players are. This plays each team's game pass by pass and carry by
    carry: how many of each, each throw's target by his share of the
    team's attempts over its last eight games (in the games he played),
    whether it is caught (his catch rate, pulled toward the league by 20
    targets), how far (his yards a catch, a gamma draw), and each carry's
    runner and yards. Three shocks touch the whole team at once — the
    number of plays, the script (passes up and runs down), and the passer's
    day (every catch likelier and longer) — and the passer's attempts,
    completions and yards are the sum of his throws, so they never disagree
    with his receivers inside one game. The shocks' sizes and the yardage
    shapes are fitted on team games (Poisson noise taken out), not chosen.
    The bar, set before its first run: every pair of two teammates' closes
    in the two newest seasons, the four outcomes priced with the closes'
    fair chances joined by the simulation's correlation and by the parlay
    pricer's (`parlays.relate`, shrunk as it shrinks), log loss averaged
    within each team-game first; it must beat the pricer with t ≥ 2 over
    300 team-games and 1,000 pairs, in each test season (CA). The pick page
    shows his over in the simulated games and which teammates' legs move
    with his ("when his hits, this hits"), beside the pricer's number; the
    game page lists where the two disagree most. Nothing on a ticket reads
    it. The builds simulate the board's teams in at most ten seconds. New
    test: `tests/test_players_inside_the_simulated_game.py` (7). Seasons
    logged before carries had the market's name read their `carries`. No
    pin changed.
133. **The inventions, part 5** (2026-10-17, the same request). (10) **A
    role change spotted early** (`engine/rolechange.py`). A role jumps — a
    receiver moves into the slot, a back takes the job — and the board
    reads volume as his last four weeks (`nflusage.VOL_WEEKS`), which keeps
    the old role in the number for three games after the new one is plain.
    Online Bayesian changepoint detection (Adams and MacKay) on each share
    series (his targets over his team's that week, his carries over his
    team's, his snap share): after every game, the chance his current role
    began 0, 1, 2 … games ago, each candidate role a normal with unknown
    mean and spread, a new role judged on what a new role would predict and
    starting with chance one in HAZARD each game. The forecast for next game
    weighs every candidate role's mean by its chance. Tested on planted
    jumps: 94% on the right game, no false alarms on 600 flat roles. The
    bar, set before its first run: the two settings chosen from a fixed
    grid on the older seasons only; every next-game share in the two newest
    seasons forecast from the games before it (four at least) by the
    detector and by the four-week average, absolute error, averaged within
    each player-season; each share must beat the average with t ≥ 2 over
    300 player-seasons and 2,000 forecasts, in each test season (CB). The
    pick page shows his shares game by game with the new role marked, how
    sure the detector is that it changed in the last three games, and its
    forecast beside the four-week average; the game page lists the players
    whose role just changed. The board's volume is unchanged. New test:
    `tests/test_a_role_change_spotted_early.py` (6). No pin changed.
134. **The inventions, part 6** (2026-10-17, the same request; all thirteen
    now built). (11) **Honest ranges** (`engine/conformal.py`): split
    conformal prediction on every graded pick in the journal — how far he
    landed from the number we published, over the square root of the
    number — gives a 50% and an 80% range around tonight's projection with
    no assumption about the shape of the misses, and a second chance for
    the pick's side (the share of past misses that would have put him over)
    beside the model's own. The bar, set before its first run: on the newest
    third of graded picks, calibrated on the oldest two thirds, each range
    covers its share within three points or two standard errors over 150
    picks (CC 1). The pick page draws the two ranges, the projection and the
    line, with both chances and the model's own range. (12) **The loss
    autopsy** (`engine/autopsy.py`): each graded NFL pick's miss split
    exactly into usage (his targets, carries or attempts against his four
    games before) and per touch, with nothing left over; each loss tagged
    left early (snaps under 60% of his usual), script (a blowout and usage
    against us), usage, per touch, or both; and each step's rate of going
    against us in losses set beside its rate in wins, so the step "wrong
    most" is named only at z ≥ 2 over 100 losses. The builds refresh it once
    a day. The pick page shows how picks in that market lose and his last
    three graded picks. (13) **One risk view of every open bet**
    (`engine/risk.py`, `/api/mybets/risk`, members only, nothing kept): My
    Bets' open tab plays the night out 5,000 times at once with every open
    leg once (the same leg on two slips is one event), our chance where a
    board prices it and its price's otherwise, legs in one game joined by
    the parlay engine's number. It shows the average night, the chance to
    finish up, the bad night (one in twenty), the chance every ticket loses
    beside what it would be if they were unrelated, where the money rides,
    and the one bet on a leg's other side that lifts the bad night most (or
    none). The settled tab keeps the report card. New tests:
    `tests/test_honest_ranges_around_every_pick.py` (5),
    `tests/test_the_loss_autopsy.py` (5),
    `tests/test_one_risk_view_of_every_open_bet.py` (5). No pin changed;
    app.js grew by the My Bets hook and stays inside its budget.

135. **The inventions in the picks, and every fit mapped** (2026-10-17,
    Ethan: "make sure we are wrapping all the features into the most likely
    bets and edge bets where it makes sense … make sure all of our tools
    and data are being used correctly and not just the ones you just added
    but ALL tools and data we were and are using"). **The gates**
    (`engine/ideagate.py`): each invention reaches the picks at the
    earliest point it belongs, so Edge and Most Likely read the same
    number, and only once its own saved verdict says HOLDS — the way
    likelycal and likelyctx already work, with no hand-wiring after a
    result. The rookie translation becomes the prior a thin rookie sample
    is pulled toward (the pick page says "his last college season,
    translated to the NFL"); the role detector scales the usage bridge's
    targets and carries, read off the same share it was measured on; the
    air becomes its own step in a kicker's chain, against the air his
    record was kicked in (half his home stadium's, half the league's) and
    never off the 65° placeholder an unforecast game carries; the simulated
    games join two teammates' parlay legs, the doc's banned pairings staying
    banned; a book's menu, the outcome recipe and a public-attention spike
    move the Most Likely chance by what they measured, together at most
    eight points, and refuse an Edge pick whose edge they take away; the
    market check uses the fair chance counted one vote per opinion; and the
    practice report and the honest range hold a pick off both boards. The
    curve, the autopsy and the risk view never move a number, by design. A
    posted pick keeps its chance, and the journal keeps the first claim. The
    NFL build now writes the readings before its Most Likely board, not
    after it, so they can reach it. Found on the way and fixed before any
    gate opens: the air step compared tonight's air with sea level (a
    Denver kicker's altitude counted twice); the simulation, once gated,
    would have been measured against itself; a pair on opposite sides would
    have taken the simulated number with the wrong sign. **The map**
    (`engine/wiremap.py`): every fitter and measurement script in the repo,
    and the thirteen, with where its answer lands — the number, the Most
    Likely board, gated, a card, a report, or failed its bar and unwired on
    purpose — checked against the code: a new fit cannot be added without
    a row, a row naming a reader that never names it fails, and nothing on
    the number path may import a fit that failed. Three findings for you,
    not changed here: the game-line haircut (the claimed moneyline edge did
    not survive in either league, and the shipped college haircut assumes
    65% does — refusing game-line Edge picks outright is your call); the
    learned multiplier model, off on purpose (the measured steps replaced
    it); and an empty, harmless usage hook on the volume markets. The
    write-up is `docs/WIRING_AUDIT_2026-10-17.md`. New tests:
    `tests/test_the_inventions_gated_into_the_picks.py` (10),
    `tests/test_every_fit_lands_where_it_says.py` (6).

Everything older and lower priority (the measurements, the Discord feed,
Kalshi + Pikkit) is in "Everything to run, in order" further down; none
of it blocks tonight.

**5. Our touchdown record against the prices we took** (re-run of T3 with

```
cd /srv/qellys && python3 - <<'PY'
import sqlite3
c = sqlite3.connect('file:data/ledger.db?mode=ro', uri=True)
rows = c.execute("""SELECT date, player, MAX(hit_prob), MAX(status='won'), MAX(odds) FROM bets
  WHERE sport='nfl' AND market='anytime_td' AND UPPER(side)='OVER'
  AND category IN ('board','likely','likely_live','matchup_td') AND status IN ('won','lost')
  GROUP BY date, player""").fetchall()
imp = lambda o: 100 / (o + 100) if o > 0 else -o / (100 - o)
for lo, hi in ((0, .3), (.3, .4), (.4, .5), (.5, .6), (.6, 1.01)):
    b = [r for r in rows if r[2] is not None and r[4] and lo <= r[2] < hi]
    if b:
        print(f"{lo:.0%}-{min(hi,1):.0%}  n {len(b):3}  we said {sum(r[2] for r in b)/len(b):.0%}  "
              f"price said {sum(imp(r[4]) for r in b)/len(b):.0%}  scored {sum(r[3] for r in b)/len(b):.0%}")
print("all:", len(rows), "picks,", sum(r[3] for r in rows), "scored")
PY
```

#### F. Kalshi + Pikkit: the site's picks placed for real, verified on Pikkit (about 20 minutes, mostly in a browser)

**ON HOLD — 2026-10-06.** Michigan has blocked every sports prediction
market (Kalshi, Polymarket, ProphetX; Novig never served Michigan), by
a court order the state calls temporary. Do not fund a Kalshi account
from Michigan. The trader is built and tested; when the court case
resolves, this section runs as written. The Michigan-legal route for a
verified record is the licensed sportsbooks (DraftKings, FanDuel,
BetMGM, Caesars), which Pikkit and Juice Reel both sync — but they ban
automated betting, so each bet is a tap by you — the **Bet it** button
(Part I) opens each pick on that book's slip. **Coinbase is not a way around it**
(checked 2026-10-06): its prediction markets are Kalshi's own contracts
routed through Kalshi's exchange, its trading API does not cover them,
and neither Pikkit nor Juice Reel syncs Coinbase.

Ethan, 2026-10-06: "link the site to a pikkit or juice reel account so we
can legitimately track all the bets the site puts in … every single edge
bet and most likely bet … We will only do nfl to start." What is built
(`engine/kalshitrade`): after every football board rebuild the box places
each pick on **Kalshi** — an exchange with an official trading API, never
a sportsbook — one contract a pick, only where Kalshi lists the same bet
at the same line. Pikkit syncs the Kalshi account by itself, so the picks
show up there as verified, nothing typed by hand. The Status page gets a
"Kalshi trader" card with the mode, today's orders and a "Verified on
Pikkit" link.

**What one contract costs.** A Kalshi contract pays $1 if the pick wins
and costs its chance in cents: a 60% pick costs about 60¢, a 35% long
shot about 35¢. Every pick is under $1; a full NFL Sunday across both
boards is roughly 60–80 picks, about $35–45. The site's units never touch
this; they stay what the record is graded on.

**What Pikkit can show.** Only what Kalshi lists: game lines match; player
props exist for the markets Kalshi carries, mostly the main games and
stats. The `run --dry` line in step 19 shows how much of a slate matches.
Still to code (blocked by the sandbox's permission filter on 2026-10-06,
retry with Ethan's go-ahead): Edge GAME bets (`game_bets`) into the edge
lane with one key per bet, prop series found on Kalshi's list by itself,
`QB_KALSHI_PRICE_RULE=any`, and defaults sized for a Sunday.

**Paper first.** In `paper` mode the box records exactly what it would
have placed, with Kalshi's price at that moment, and places nothing. Two
days of that, we read the report together, then `live`.

**17. The two accounts (browser).**
   a. **Kalshi**: sign up at https://kalshi.com, verify your identity,
      fund it (free ACH; $50 covers about one Sunday of every pick). Then
      *Account → Settings → API Keys → Create key*. It shows a **Key ID**
      and downloads a **private key file** (text starting
      `-----BEGIN PRIVATE KEY-----`). Keep both; the file goes on the
      box in step 18 and is never pasted anywhere else.
   b. **Pikkit**: in the app, use (or make) the account that will hold
      the site's record, *Connect a sportsbook → Kalshi*, log in. Copy
      your Pikkit profile link (the `links.pikkit.com/user/...` one).

**18. The key and the settings on the box.** The file first — this
pastes it straight into place with no editor: run the first line, paste
the WHOLE key file (every line from BEGIN to END), press Enter, then
**Ctrl-D**:

```
sudo sh -c 'umask 077; cat > /etc/qellys/kalshi.pem'
sudo chown qellys:qellys /etc/qellys/kalshi.pem && sudo chmod 600 /etc/qellys/kalshi.pem && sudo head -c 27 /etc/qellys/kalshi.pem; echo
```

(That prints `-----BEGIN PRIVATE KEY----` and nothing more.) Then the key
id, the Pikkit link (each prompts; paste at the prompt), paper mode and
the every-bet settings — every Most Likely tier and every staked Edge
pick, NFL only, 150 orders and $50 a day at most, paying up to 3¢ over
our own chance:

```
cd /srv/qellys && sudo ./deploy/setenv.sh QB_KALSHI_KEY_ID
sudo ./deploy/setenv.sh QB_PIKKIT_MODEL_URL
sudo ./deploy/setenv.sh QB_KALSHI_MODE paper
sudo ./deploy/setenv.sh QB_KALSHI_LANES potd,top,strong,look,edge,game
sudo ./deploy/setenv.sh QB_KALSHI_MAX_ORDERS_DAY 150
sudo ./deploy/setenv.sh QB_KALSHI_DAILY_CAP_CENTS 5000
sudo ./deploy/setenv.sh QB_KALSHI_SLACK_CENTS 3
sudo ./deploy/setenv.sh QB_KALSHI_RESERVE_CENTS 200
sudo systemctl restart qellys
```

**19. Prove it, place nothing.** `check` signs one read-only request and
prints `ok — connected; balance $…`; `run --dry` prints every pick it
would place right now, the Kalshi market it matched and the price, and
writes nothing. Paste both:

```
cd /srv/qellys && sudo -u qellys env $(sudo cat /etc/qellys/env | grep -E '^QB_(KALSHI|PIKKIT)' | xargs) python3 -m engine.kalshitrade check
cd /srv/qellys && sudo -u qellys env $(sudo cat /etc/qellys/env | grep -E '^QB_(KALSHI|PIKKIT)' | xargs) python3 -m engine.kalshitrade run --dry
```

`FAILED — HTTP 401` means the key id and the file do not belong together
(make the key again and redo step 18). `no Kalshi market for this bet`
on a prop is expected until step 20.

lines match already, props need their series named in
QB_KALSHI_PROP_SERIES and I set that from this list):


every football rebuild. Then paste the report and we look at it together:

```
cd /srv/qellys && sudo -u qellys env $(sudo cat /etc/qellys/env | grep -E '^QB_(KALSHI|PIKKIT)' | xargs) python3 -m engine.kalshitrade report
```

**22. Live — only after step 21, and only when you say so:**

